Note on Colour Convention
Text in blue italic throughout this document represents analyst-generated descriptions of screen fields and behaviour, derived by interpreting the session screenshots - not verbatim from the requirements discussion. Text in dark teal marks the Layman Description subsections added in version 0.11. These restate each capability in everyday language with a worked example, for readers who need the substance without the specification detail. They are explanatory only and create no requirement.

{Heading1} 1. Introduction
{Heading2} 1.1 Purpose
This document defines the functional requirements for the Warehouse Management System (WMS) - Inward Module of the Sparecare integrated platform. It is derived from seven requirements discussion sessions held on August 4 and August 12, 2026, between the Sparecare product team (Naveen Chauhan, Santosh) and Deepmindz. The document translates session discussions and referenced wireframe screens into structured functional specifications for WMS wireframe design and development.
{Heading2} 1.2 Scope
This FRD covers the following areas from Sessions 01 to 07:
{ListParagraph} System context - Sparecare four existing operating domains and the strategic direction towards a unified integrated cloud platform
{ListParagraph} WMS as the fifth module - its position within the Sparecare ecosystem and five broad sub-modules
{ListParagraph} Inward Module - end-to-end lifecycle from truck arrival through ERP/DMS synchronisation and finance advice
{ListParagraph} Gate Entry (Screen 1) - GEN creation, configurable field capture, document photo upload
{ListParagraph} Consignment Details &amp; Workflow Configuration (Screen 2) - product/supplier classification, GEN linkage, per-consignment workflow step applicability
{ListParagraph} Inward Operations Dashboard (Screen 3) - real-time pending queue visibility
{ListParagraph} Receipt Details - Invoice &amp; Transporter Capture (Screens 4a &amp; 4b) - document number, invoice capture with line-level detail, transporter and driver information, document upload
{ListParagraph} Truck Unload Initiation &amp; GEN Selection (Screens 5A &amp; 5b) - GEN search and autofill, invoice selection, transporter verification
{ListParagraph} Box Scanning, Condition Assessment &amp; Variance Check (Screens 6a, 6b &amp; 6c) - box-by-box QR scan, condition recording, open delivery workflow, unloading WIP tracker, variance summary
{ListParagraph} Extended unloading behaviour (Screens 6a, 6b &amp; 6c) - mid-session save and resume, ship list view and replacement, ship list mismatch handling, unload progress and exception summary, printable unload summary
{ListParagraph} Unloading Without Shipment Document (Screens 6d, 6e &amp; 6f) - primary code scan or part number entry, box-number-wise unloading, admin-configured unload approach and manual box counter
{ListParagraph} Box Sticker Generation for Non-Scannable Consignments (Screens 6g &amp; 6h) - system-generated temporary box references, sticker printing and scan-back
{ListParagraph} Consignment Check Initiation (Screen 7a) - GEN-based retrieval of an unloaded consignment and routing between full consignment check and Direct to Bin
{ListParagraph} Direct to Bin - Secondary Scan &amp; Product Identification (Screens 8a &amp; 8b) - secondary box scan, product master lookup, fragile flagging, Issue for Usage, Bin Later batching, D2B and D2W sticker printing
{ListParagraph} Bin Placement &amp; Location Confirmation (Screen 8c) - location scan or manual confirmation, part scan, quantity capture and wrong-location handling
{ListParagraph} Direct Binning Case 1A (Screens 8A, 8B, 8C &amp; 8D) - primary and secondary cartons the same, unit-by-unit product inspection, damaged-unit set aside for insurance, two-step binning verification, wrong-location override and the bin-later queue
{ListParagraph} Direct Binning Case 1B (Screens 9a, 9b and 9A, 9B &amp; 9C) - single SKU in a primary carton where the primary-product linkage is unavailable, manual quantity capture, condition check and routing, and the multiple-SKU and no-location exception paths
{ListParagraph} Direct Binning Case 1C (Screens 10A, 10B, 10C &amp; 10D) - primary and secondary cartons the same with only the secondary product scannable, box reference sticker option, secondary scan against master data, unit-by-unit inspection and two-step verified binning
{ListParagraph} Direct Binning Case 1D (Screens 10E, 10F, 10G &amp; 10H) - neither carton nor product scannable and no supplier data, manual and predictive product identification, physical count entry, and the exception and next-state panel
{ListParagraph} Case selection and cross-cutting condition rules - screen configuration driven by the supplier and product combination, default OK condition on a collapsed panel, and the relabelling of MOQ as quantity per pack size
{ListParagraph} Video recording of carton opening - optional toggle at the start of a secondary carton check and a retain-or-delete prompt on completion
{ListParagraph} Open Delivery Flow, Case 1 - inspection of cartons flagged during unloading, piece-to-piece condition capture, and binning of good pieces only
{ListParagraph} Multi-SKU Consignment Check (Screens 11a &amp; 11b) - a primary carton holding several SKUs, repeated secondary scanning against a constant primary box, and box-level reconciliation against the ship list
{ListParagraph} Consignment Check Variance Summary (Screen 11c) - invoiced against received in value, part lines and quantity with short, excess and net, and quality variance by qualitative aspect
{ListParagraph} Case 2A (Screens 11A, 11B &amp; 11C) - secondary cartons inside the primary carton with both scannable and linkage available, per-SKU expected against scanned tracking, and carton-wise variance reporting
{ListParagraph} Case 2B (six-step flow) - secondary cartons inside the primary carton with both scannable but no linkage, the linkage established by the physical check, actual-scanned work-in-progress counts, and a consignment summary without carton-level variance
{ListParagraph} Case 2C (six-step flow) - only the secondary product scannable, the box reference sticker option or the opened cartons counter, and the same summary treatment as Case 2B
{ListParagraph} Case 2D (Screens 13A, 13B, 13C &amp; 13D) - nothing scannable and no supplier details, identification by printed barcode stickers or manual and predictive search, condition captured for the selected product only, and the exception and next-state panel
{ListParagraph} Open Delivery extension - primary carton capture with smudged-code handling, duplicate scan blocking for unique codes, unique scan code generation for non-unique codes, and bin location selection where several locations are permitted
{ListParagraph} Binning - cart assembly (Screens 12a &amp; 12b) - product scanning into a cart, quantity accumulation, the fragile indicator, cart capacity held outside the system, and the cart-then-location-then-parts structure
{ListParagraph} Binning execution (Screens 13a, 13b, 14a, 14b &amp; 15) - binning by product, binning by location and direct binning, with location and part verification at the bin, partial placement and relocation
{ListParagraph} Consignment workflow tracking and system action traceability - a graphical stepper by consignment or ship list reference, stage durations, and a trace of stickers, generated codes and manual entries
{ListParagraph} Location Audit - the two audit approaches introduced at the close of Session 06 for detailing in a subsequent session
{ListParagraph} Nature of inward transactions - purchase, branch transfer in, sales return, workshop or in-house return, job work return, and third party purchase with contractor-owned stock held separately from the business's own inventory
{ListParagraph} Consignment master data and category management - supplier, carrier, product and document classifications, the mandatory-description Others fallback, auto-generated category codes, PIN code lookup, bulk upload, and inactive categories with the move or don't-move decision
{ListParagraph} Mobile and web channel coverage - explicit support for both surfaces and a screen-by-screen assignment of which surface each screen is delivered on
{ListParagraph} Ship list capture - soft copy upload, page-by-page camera capture assembled into a PDF, extraction into the master data tables, and view and replace after upload
{ListParagraph} Warehouse location planning and wave picking, recorded as forward-looking capability outside the current inward scope
{ListParagraph} Amendments closing gaps against the BRD - gate entry date and time of receipt, dashboard aging buckets, document validation at unload selection, auto-finish confirmation with a decline path and partial-unload recording, the third plain-count unload option, permission-gated binning where no location exists, and administrator control over manual entry with scan compliance reporting
{ListParagraph} Role hierarchy, access control and audit logging - the Sparecare solution administrator, dealer administrator and dealer user roles, with auditable logs created for every action by every role
{ListParagraph} Terminology standardisation - withdrawal of the term Inward Code in favour of the Gate Entry Number throughout the document and the wireframes, and correction of the Courier Type value list
{ListParagraph} Corrections to analyst-generated field descriptions on Screen 1 - the truck driver as the sole subject of the name and contact fields, and supplier notification derived from the supplier record rather than the driver's number
{ListParagraph} System module context - the five-module breakdown of the warehouse management system with Phase 1 confined to Module 1, Receiving (Inward)
{ListParagraph} Corrections to the receipt details specification - the invoice summary table located on Screen 4b as the summary panel, with invoice entry remaining on Screen 4a
{ListParagraph} Correction to gate entry selection - the find-by-other-details lookup as a search panel within Screen 5A rather than a separate screen, offering search criteria only
{ListParagraph} The Check Unloading Status search as a read-only binary lookup - unloaded or not unloaded, no condition editing, no variance, and available after the unload session has closed
{ListParagraph} Photographic evidence made mandatory where a box condition other than OK is recorded, and not required where the condition is OK
{ListParagraph} Reconciliation baseline for unloading fixed on the expected box count recorded at Receipt Details, with the gate entry box count treated as indicative only
{ListParagraph} Binning location exception handling (Screens 16A to 16F) - the mapped location full, no alternate mapped location available, and no bin location defined, with alternate selection, RBAC-gated manual entry, temporary location and administrator request
{ListParagraph} Administrator location exception queue (Screen 17) - the exception backlog by type, request detail, location assignment against the zone hierarchy, temporary manual binning, write-back to the part-location master, and publish-and-notify
{ListParagraph} Location recommendation and alternate location rules - the derivation bases put forward, the position that location capacity is not captured, and operator declaration as the sole means of recording a location full
{ListParagraph} Forward-looking binning capabilities - schematic site map, voice interaction, cart movement tracking, cart splitting across locations, and system-directed cart composition
Steps 7 (ERP/DMS Sync), 8 (Finance Advice), and sub-modules beyond Inward will be covered in subsequent FRD sessions.
{Heading2} 1.3 Definitions &amp; Abbreviations
[TABLE]
| Term | Definition |
| WMS | Warehouse Management System - the fifth module being added to the Sparecare integrated platform, covering physical goods receipt, storage, and dispatch operations |
| GEN | Gate Entry Number - a unique system-generated reference code assigned to every consignment arriving at the warehouse. Serves as the master reference key for the entire inward journey |
| Inward Module | The first phase of WMS scope; covers the end-to-end process from truck arrival at the gate through to ERP/DMS sync and finance advice |
| GRN | Goods Receipt Note - a document confirming receipt of goods, one of the accepted primary document types at gate entry |
| Challan | A delivery note or dispatch document issued by the supplier, accepted as a primary document at gate entry |
| Ship List | A box-level manifest issued by the OEM detailing each primary box number and its contents; content varies by OEM brand |
| OEM | Original Equipment Manufacturer - a brand principal that dispatches spare parts to dealer locations |
| DMS | Dealer Management System - the dealer-side ERP/DMS that receives stock updates after the inward process is complete |
| ERP | Enterprise Resource Planning system - the backend system that receives stock and financial data post-inward |
| Primary Box | The outer shipping carton received from the supplier; its quantity and condition are checked at the Truck Unload stage |
| Secondary Box | The inner product packaging found inside a primary box; checked during the Consignment Check stage |
| Binning | The process of assigning received stock items to specific warehouse storage locations |
| VOR Order | Vehicle Off-Road Order - an urgent spare parts order requiring immediate fulfilment; typically single-part, single-box |
| STO | Stock Transfer Order - an internal transfer of parts from one dealer branch to another |
| SIMS | Sparecare Inventory Management System - existing cloud-based platform for replenishment order management |
| Gainer | Existing Sparecare inventory-sharing application for intra/inter-dealer parts sharing and liquidation |
| AccuStock | Existing Sparecare desktop-based audit services platform for physical inventory audits |
| LR | Lorry Receipt - a transport document issued by the transporter acknowledging receipt of goods for delivery |
| SKU | Stock Keeping Unit - a unique identifier for an individual product or part number |
| WIP | Work In Progress - in the context of truck unload, refers to the real-time unloading status tracker showing progress across all boxes |
| Open Delivery | A process in which a damaged or suspect box is opened and inspected in front of the transporter/driver, and the condition is recorded for claims purposes |
| Box Mismatch | A situation during unloading where a scanned box does not match the expected delivery manifest for the GEN - requires confirmation or rejection by the warehouse user |
| Quality Variance | A summary report of box condition discrepancies per consignment: counts of Damage, Tampered Packaging, Scratch, Broken, and Wrong Delivery boxes |
| Consignment Check | The secondary verification stage performed after the truck has been unloaded and has left the premises. Primary boxes are opened and their contents verified against the expected quantity. |
| Direct to Bin (D2B) | A routing decision by which a received part is sent straight to its storage bin without a separate secondary consignment check. Applied where the primary and secondary box are the same physical unit. |
| Direct to Workshop (D2W) | A routing decision by which a received part is sent straight to the workshop against a vehicle awaiting that part, bypassing storage. |
| IForU / I4U | Issue for Usage - a flag raised when a scanned part matches an open workshop demand, indicating the part is to be segregated for immediate issue rather than binned. |
| FR (Fragile) | Fragile indicator held against the product master. Activated on screen when a part defined as fragile is scanned, signalling that it requires careful handling. |
| MRP Sticker | The secondary box sticker carrying the part identifier and price. It is physically attached to the primary packing, which is why it is lost when the packing is torn off. |
| Bin Later | An action that defers binning of a scanned part so that several parts can be accumulated and binned together in a single trip. |
| Binning Zone | The warehouse area allocated to a part in the product master, for example a rack block or aisle group. |
| Binning Location | The specific rack, shelf or bin position within a binning zone at which a part is to be stored. |
| MOQ | Minimum Order Quantity - the smallest quantity in which a part is ordered or issued; displayed during binning as a handling reference. |
| Use Counter | A manual increment control used to count boxes as they are unloaded when neither a ship list nor a scannable box code is available. |
| Box Reference (Temporary) | A system-generated box number in the format BX-nnnn, issued when no supplier box number exists. Recorded as system-generated so it is not treated as a supplier manifest reference downstream. |
| Configured Unload Approach | The unloading method selected by the dealer admin for a given scenario - scan-based, counter-based, or sticker-print-and-scan. |
| Case 1A | The direct binning scenario in which both the primary carton and the secondary unit are scannable, supplier data has been uploaded, and the primary-secondary linkage is available, so the system knows what and how much the carton should contain. |
| Case 1B | The direct binning scenario in which supplier carton and product data exist but the linkage between them is unavailable. The carton may hold only one SKU, and quantity is established by physical scan or count. |
| Primary Carton | The outer carton bearing the primary carton barcode. Used interchangeably with primary box in the unloading sections. |
| Secondary Unit | The individual packaged product inside a primary carton, bearing the product code on the unit or its secondary packing. |
| Primary-Product Linkage | Supplier-provided data associating a primary carton with the product and quantity it contains. Where unavailable, expected quantity cannot be derived and must be captured by count. |
| Recommended Location | The bin location the system proposes for a part, drawn from the product master. |
| Alternative / Other Allowed Locations | Additional bin locations at which a part may legitimately be stored, offered where the recommended location is full or otherwise unavailable. |
| Set Aside (Insurance) | The outcome applied to a unit found damaged during inspection. The unit is segregated rather than binned so that an insurance claim can be raised against it. |
| Check Carton | The exception process to which a carton is routed when direct binning cannot proceed, most commonly when multiple product codes are detected inside a single carton. |
| Capture Status | Indicator on the primary carton record showing how far identification has progressed, for example PRIMARY CAPTURED. |
| Bin Later Queue | The list of cartons or parts marked for deferred binning, showing the count pending. Deferred binning is completed through the Binning module. |
| Case 1C | Primary and secondary cartons are the same physical unit and only the secondary product is scannable. Secondary product details are uploaded but the primary-secondary linkage is not available, so the carton is referenced either by a printed box reference sticker or not at all. |
| Case 1D | Primary and secondary cartons are the same physical unit and nothing is scannable. No supplier details are available and no linkage exists, so the product is identified by manual or predictive search and the quantity is counted physically. |
| Case 2A | Secondary cartons are contained inside the primary carton. Both are scannable, supplier details are uploaded and the primary-secondary linkage is available, so the check reconciles what is found against what the ship list states for each carton. |
| Pack Size | The number of units contained in one sale pack, held as master data against the product. This is the value currently displayed on screen as MOQ; the label is to be corrected. |
| Multi-SKU Primary Carton | A primary carton holding more than one product code. The primary box is scanned once and each secondary product inside it is scanned in turn, with the primary box held constant. |
| Check Variance | The closing step of a consignment check. Reports invoiced against received in value, part lines and quantity with short, excess and net positions, and reports quality variance by qualitative aspect. |
| Piece-to-Piece Inspection | Inspection of every individual unit in a carton, used on the open delivery path where the carton was flagged during unloading. Good pieces are binned and damaged pieces are set aside for insurance. |
| Exception / Next State | The panel presenting the states that arise when a carton cannot be completed normally - the no location alert state and the bin later state - together with the actions available from each. |
| Case 2B | Secondary cartons inside the primary carton where both are scannable and supplier details are uploaded, but the primary-secondary linkage is not available. The linkage is created by the physical check itself, and no carton-level variance can be reported. |
| Case 2C | Secondary cartons inside the primary carton where only the secondary product is scannable and no linkage exists. The primary carton is either given a printed box reference sticker or simply counted as opened. |
| Case 2D | Secondary cartons inside the primary carton where nothing is scannable, no supplier details are available and no linkage exists. Products are identified by manual or predictive search or by printed barcode stickers, and quantities are counted physically. |
| Cart (Binning Cart) | The physical cart or trolley into which products are collected for binning. Items are scanned as they are added, the same product scanned again increasing the quantity on that line. One cart may serve more than one bin location. |
| Binning by Product | The binning route that starts from the product: a product is selected from the cart and the system directs the operator to the location recorded against it. |
| Binning by Location | The binning route that starts from the location: a location is selected and the system shows which product from the cart belongs there. |
| Direct Binning | Placement of a single product without reference to a cart, capturing product, zone and location and confirming both at the bin. |
| Unique Scan Code | A code generated and printed by the system for affixing to a secondary pack where the supplier's own code is not unique to the unit, so that individual units can be tracked. |
| Opened Cartons Counter | A count of how many primary cartons have been opened for checking, used where cartons cannot be identified individually. It advances only when the operator confirms the current carton is complete. |
| Location Audit | The checking of parts held at a warehouse location, either against a preassigned plan of locations or parts, or generically without planning. |
| Branch Transfer IN | An inward transaction covering goods transferred from another warehouse within the same business, rather than purchased from an external supplier. |
| Sales Return | An inward transaction covering goods returned by a customer, whether because the product was not up to standard or for another recorded reason. |
| Workshop / In-house Return | An inward transaction covering parts previously issued for usage and returned unconsumed - for example where a workshop drew five units and used three. |
| Job Work Return | An inward transaction covering goods returning from an outward job work transaction, where a process the service centre cannot perform - welding, polishing or milling - was carried out by a third party. |
| Third Party Purchase | An inward transaction covering goods belonging to a contractor operating within the customer's premises. Ownership rests with the contractor, and the stock is held separately from the business's own inventory. |
| Others Fallback | The default category held at the end of every classification list, used where an item is not covered by any defined category. Selecting it requires a description, so that recurring entries can later be promoted into categories of their own. |
| Inactive Category | A category retired from use without being deleted, so that the historical data attached to it is preserved. On deactivation the user nominates a destination category for existing records or chooses Don't Move. |
| Aging Bucket | A time band applied to the pending queues on the inward dashboard - last 7, 15, 30 and 60-plus days - allowing the operator to see which pending work has been outstanding longest. |
| Scan Compliance | The proportion of a load binned by scanning against the proportion entered manually, reported so that the exposure created by manual entry is visible. |
| Sparecare Solution Administrator | The top role in the access hierarchy, administering the platform across dealer organisations. |
| Dealer Administrator | The role that configures the system for a single dealer organisation - field visibility, category masters, workflow defaults and user permissions. |
| Dealer User | The role that carries out warehouse operations within the permissions granted by the dealer administrator. |
| Auditable Log | A record of an action taken in the system, capturing what was done, by whom and when. Created for every action by every role, not only for configuration changes. |
| Module 1 - Receiving (Inward) | The phase 1 scope of this document: truck arrival, goods receipt note creation, quality and quantity checking, binning, stock movement, ERP and DMS synchronisation, and finance advice. |
| Check Unloading Status | A read-only lookup on Screen 6b taking a box number or part number and returning whether that item has been unloaded. Returns a binary result only; carries no condition editing and no variance information, and remains available after the unload session has closed. |
| Expected Box Count | The number of boxes recorded as No. of Boxes in GEN at the Receipt Details stage on Screen 4a. The authoritative baseline against which physical unloading is reconciled. Distinct from the indicative box count that may be taken at gate entry. |
| Location Exception | A condition at binning in which the location recorded against a part cannot receive the stock: the mapped location is full, no alternate mapped location is available, or no bin location is defined for the part. |
| Alternate Mapped Location | A location other than the recommended one that is recorded against the part in the master and may receive its stock. Distinct from a location entered manually outside the mapped list, which requires authorisation. |
| Temporary Location | A holding location recorded against stock that has no defined bin location, used so that binning can proceed while the administrator settles the master. |
| Part-Location Master | The mapping of parts to bin locations from which recommended and alternate locations are drawn. Updatable by the administrator when resolving a location exception. |
[/TABLE]
{Heading1} 2. System Context &amp; Application Vision
{Heading2} 2.1 Sparecare Application Overview
Sparecare currently serves automotive dealer customers across four operating domains, each supported by a distinct application:
{ListParagraph} Inventory Management (SIMS) - replenishment orders shared with customers to support multi-SKU inventory planning
{ListParagraph} Operational Excellence (Power BI) - gap identification, action tracking, and inventory performance dashboards
{ListParagraph} Inventory Sharing (Gainer) - intra/inter-dealer parts sharing, slow-mover identification, and liquidation support
{ListParagraph} Audit Services (AccuStock) - inventory and process audits at client locations through multiple field approaches
The target state is one integrated cloud application on a common database with modular SaaS architecture, delivered in phases. Currently SIMS and Gainer are cloud-hosted; AccuStock is desktop-based. The new unified platform consolidates workflows and reporting across all domains.
 [IMG]
Figure 2.1a - Sparecare Application Vision: four operating domains and target state (one integrated cloud application, common database, modular SaaS architecture)
{Heading2} 2.2 WMS as the Fifth Module
The Warehouse Management System (WMS) is the fifth module to be added to the Sparecare integrated platform. It is being built on a new modular architecture to address limitations of the current legacy ASP.NET R12 platform (SCOPE). The WMS will initially focus on the physical goods receipt workflow (Inward Module) and will progressively expand to cover outward, returns, and data analytics.
{Heading2} 2.3 WMS Module Breakdown
The WMS comprises five broad modules:
{ListParagraph} Module 1 - Receiving (Inward): truck arrival, GRN, quality and quantity check, binning, stock movement, ERP/DMS sync, finance advice
{ListParagraph} Module 2 - Operational Excellence: real-time tracking, cycle count / wall-to-wall audit, discrepancy flagging, adjustments, audit trail
{ListParagraph} Module 3 - Outward: sales/service order fulfilment, pick list generation, picking via barcode/RFID, packing and labelling, dispatch and gate pass
{ListParagraph} Module 4 - Returns: return request handling, goods received, inspection and categorisation, inventory update / disposal
{ListParagraph} Module 5 - Data &amp; Analytics: KPI reports, dashboards and analytics, alerts and notifications
Phase 1 scope (this FRD) focuses on Module 1 - Receiving (Inward). The entire WMS will operate on handheld terminals (rugged mobile devices) to enable warehouse staff to scan, record, and update data at the point of physical activity.
 [IMG]
Figure 2.3a - WMS Module Breakdown: five-column flow diagram showing all modules with their constituent steps
{Heading2} 2.4 Note on Module Coverage (Session 07)
A caveat was recorded during this session about how much of the wider system has actually been captured. The five-module breakdown at section 2.3 was described in the earlier sessions at overview level only; the detail beneath each of Modules 2 to 5 was not walked through, and individual options within them were mentioned in passing rather than specified.
What was not stated in those sessions is therefore not in this document. The module descriptions for Modules 2 to 5 will need to be gone through in their own right before those phases are specified, and their absence here should not be read as a decision to exclude them.
Note: this matters for reading the rest of the document. Where a capability touches outward movement, returns or analytics, it appears here only to the extent it was raised while discussing inward. Absence from this document is not evidence that a capability is out of scope for the product.
{Heading1} 3. Inward Module Overview
{Heading2} 3.1 Module Purpose &amp; Scope
The Inward Module covers the complete lifecycle of a consignment from the moment it reaches the warehouse gate until the stock is updated in the system and financial advice is generated. The end result is: controlled physical inward + digital inventory accuracy + downstream system readiness.
 [IMG]
Figure 3.1a - Inward Module Overview: eight-step process with three control phases (Operational Control, Warehouse Execution, System Closure)
{Heading2} 3.2 Inward Process Steps
[TABLE]
| Step | Name | Description |
| 01 | Gate Entry | Arrival of consignment at the warehouse gate. GEN created as master reference. |
| 02 | Receipt Details | Document and supplier data capture. Invoice details, transporter info, and box/invoice counts confirmed. |
| 03 | Truck Unload | Primary carton quality and quantity check. Physical boxes scanned, conditions assessed. |
| 04 | Consignment Check | Inside-box product check. Secondary boxes opened; parts inspected and condition verified. |
| 05 | Binning | Stock items assigned to specific warehouse bin/location. System-suggested or manually overridden. |
| 06 | Stock Movement | Physical movement to assigned locations. Location update recorded. |
| 07 | ERP / DMS Sync | System reconciliation. Stock data synchronised with dealer ERP/DMS. |
| 08 | Finance Advice | Post-receipt payment processing. Financial advice generated where applicable. |
[/TABLE]
{Heading2} 3.3 Consignment Source Types
The WMS must handle consignments from four distinct source types:
{ListParagraph} OEM Dispatch - material dispatched by a brand principal. May come with ship list, invoice, or GRN depending on OEM brand.
{ListParagraph} Branch / STO - material transferred from the dealer own branch. Typically single box; secondary check and binning may be skipped.
{ListParagraph} Co-Dealer / Distributor - material from another dealer or authorised distributor. May come on invoice or delivery challan.
{ListParagraph} Local / Retail - parts sourced locally from a retail point or vendor.
{Heading2} 3.4 OEM Document Variability
A key design consideration is that the document accompanying an OEM consignment varies by brand:
{ListParagraph} Some OEMs (e.g., Maruti) provide a ship list - a box-level manifest with each primary box number and its contents, with matching soft copy.
{ListParagraph} Other OEMs provide only an invoice stating total box count and an itemised parts list.
{ListParagraph} Where OEM ship list or GRN data is available in the back end, the system shall automatically populate invoice numbers in the Screen 4a invoice table (brand-by-brand configuration).
{ListParagraph} Document content, format, and delivery channel vary by brand. The system must support configurable document type mapping per OEM brand.
{Heading1} 4. FR-001: Gate Entry (Create GEN) - Screen 1
{Heading2} 4.1 Overview
The Gate Entry screen is the first step in the inward process. It captures minimum required information and generates the Gate Entry Number (GEN) that serves as the master reference for all subsequent inward stages. Only two fields are mandatory; all others are dealer-configurable.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Gate Entry Creation (FR-001.1 to .11) - Most warehouses already keep a paper register at the gate - vehicle number, driver name, time in. This does the same job, but in the system, and it does not stay at the gate. The moment a vehicle reaches the gate someone creates an entry for it, and the system gives that entry its own number. That number then stays with the load for the rest of its life in the warehouse. Every step afterwards - unloading, checking the boxes, putting them on the shelves - is recorded against it. The value shows up weeks later, when something is queried. Instead of asking three people and hunting through a paper book, you type one number and get the whole history. It also means two loads from the same supplier on the same day never get mixed up, because each one carries its own number.
Example: A truck reaches the gate at 9:40 am. The guard opens the system, enters the vehicle number and the driver name, and saves. The system gives it GE-1042 and prints a slip. The truck goes to bay 3, 48 boxes come off, two are found torn, and all of it is recorded under GE-1042. Three days later accounts find a damaged headlamp and ask which vehicle brought it. The storekeeper types GE-1042 and sees the arrival time, the driver, who unloaded it and that two boxes were torn on arrival - enough to raise the claim with the supplier.
Gate Entry Creation (FR-001.12; FR-001.21) - Recording a load is split into two stages, because the two places have different amounts of time and different information. At the gate the guard has a driver waiting and a queue behind him. He is not going to open cartons or count 200 boxes. He takes the few things he can see - who is driving, a phone number, the vehicle, roughly what has come - and lets the vehicle in. The real detail is captured later at the unloading dock, by the storekeeper who has the paperwork in his hand and the boxes in front of him. That is where invoices are entered and the actual box count is recorded. The distinction matters because it decides which number the warehouse trusts. Anything noted at the gate is a rough glance and should never be used to argue a shortage. The figure recorded at the dock is the one everything else is checked against.
Example: The guard notes driver Ramesh, phone 98xxx, vehicle MH-12-4456 and roughly 50 boxes, then lets the truck in. Twenty minutes later at the dock the storekeeper opens the envelope, enters 3 invoices and records 48 boxes expected. During unloading the men scan 48 and nothing is short. Had the system treated the guard rough figure of 50 as the expected count, it would have reported 2 boxes missing that were never on the truck in the first place.
Gate Entry Creation (FR-001.16 to .20) - Two things are captured here. First, who physically brought the load and how to reach him afterwards - his name and phone number. Second, how it came: your own vehicle, a courier company, a transporter, or a person carrying it by hand. The driver detail matters when something is found wrong after the vehicle has gone. Without a name and a number, a damaged carton becomes an argument with nobody to ask. The transport type matters for a different reason: it tells you which loads arrive by which route, and it is exactly the kind of list that keeps changing as the business changes. So the question is not only whether the field exists, but who controls the list behind it. If your own administrator can add a new courier firm, you do it the same afternoon. If the list is fixed inside the software, you raise a request with the vendor and wait.
Example: A carton is found wet the next morning. The record shows driver Ramesh on 98xxx, arriving by courier, so the storekeeper calls him before the insurance claim goes in and learns it rained during loading. Separately, the branch starts using a new courier firm in March. The administrator adds it to the transport list that same afternoon, and the next arrival is recorded correctly - no ticket raised, nobody waiting.
{Heading2} 4.2 Functional Requirements
FR-001.1: The system shall generate a unique Gate Entry Number (GEN) upon submission of the gate entry form. The GEN shall serve as the master reference key for all subsequent inward process steps.
FR-001.2: The gate entry form shall require a minimum of two mandatory fields: Name of the Person and Contact Number. All other fields shall be optional and configurable by the dealer admin.
FR-001.3: The dealer admin shall be able to configure which fields appear on the gate entry screen for their location(s). Fields not required can be hidden entirely.
FR-001.4: The system shall accept any type of incoming consignment through the gate entry - not limited to spare parts. Any goods for which the dealer organisation is making payment shall be eligible for a GEN.
FR-001.5: The gate entry screen shall support capture of a primary document via a camera/upload interface. Document capture at Screen 1 is optional; it may be completed at Screen 2 (Receipt Details).
FR-001.6: All data entered on Screen 1 that is not captured shall be editable and completable at Screen 2. No information shall become permanently locked before Screen 2 is accessed.
FR-001.7: The For Whom / Department Name field, when populated, shall trigger a system notification to the named department informing them of the consignment arrival.
FR-001.8: The GEN shall be displayed prominently on screen after creation (green banner) and shall be printable or shareable as a reference.
{Heading2} 4.3 Screen Field Reference
Note: The following table describes Screen 1 (Create GEN) fields as observed in the wireframe. Descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |
| Name of the Person | Text input | Mandatory field. Captures the name of the truck driver only. The gate personnel are a separate role and are not recorded in this field. |
| Contact Number | Text input | Mandatory field. Mobile or phone number of the truck driver. Not linked to the supplier record and not used to notify the supplier. |
| Where From (Supplier Name) | Dropdown | Optional. Selects the source supplier from the pre-configured supplier master list. Can be completed at Screen 2 if unknown at gate. |
| For Whom (Department Name) | Dropdown | Optional. Department or individual for whom the consignment is intended. Triggers internal arrival notification when populated. |
| Primary Document Type | Dropdown + Camera | Optional at gate. Acceptable types: GRN, Tax Invoice, Challan, Ship List, Delivery Challan, others as configured. Camera enables document photo capture. |
| Courier Type | Dropdown | Optional. Mode of transport: Transport / Courier / Own Vehicle / Individual (External Person) / Individual (Internal Person), per the BRD. Configurable from master data. |
| Gate Entry Number (GEN) | System-generated display | Read-only. Auto-generated on Next. Displayed in green banner at bottom of form. Master reference for all subsequent inward steps. |
[/TABLE]
{Heading2} 4.4 Amendments from Session 07
The gate entry screen was reviewed against the BRD and two omissions were identified. The first is the date and time of receipt. The BRD requires the gate entry to carry the date and time at which the consignment was received, automatically picked up by the system, and treats it as a mandatory, non-negotiable capture. The FRD as written names only the person's name and contact number as mandatory fields and carries no date or time row at all. The two are different in kind: the name and contact number are dealer-configurable mandatory fields, whereas the date and time of receipt is captured by the system and is not subject to configuration.
The second is the nature of the inward transaction. The gate entry is the point at which a consignment enters the business premises, and the reason it is entering determines much of what follows. The six transaction types are set out in section 33.
Two further points were confirmed about the gate entry number itself. It is created for every consignment received, it signifies that the consignment has entered the business premises, and every subsequent activity on that consignment is conducted by reference to it. It is also the reference against which the business makes payment to the vendor, which connects the gate entry to the payment advice raised to finance for goods received on post-receipt payment terms. The generation logic for the gate entry number has not yet been defined.
Note: the distinction between system-captured and dealer-configurable mandatory fields matters for the screen design. The date and time of receipt should be displayed on Screen 1 as a read-only, system-populated value rather than as an input the user can leave blank or edit.
FR-001.9: The gate entry shall capture the date and time of receipt of the consignment. The value shall be picked up automatically by the system at the moment the gate entry is created.
FR-001.10: The date and time of receipt shall be mandatory and shall not be configurable. It is distinct from the dealer-configurable mandatory fields and shall not be subject to the same configuration.
FR-001.11: The gate entry shall capture the nature of the inward transaction, selected from the six types defined in section 33.
FR-001.12: The administrator shall be able to customise which details are captured at gate entry. The capturable set shall include vendor name, category of products, name of the person carrying the consignment, courier name where applicable, reference document number, document date and number of boxes in the consignment.
FR-001.13: The gate entry number shall serve as the reference against which the business makes payment to the vendor, and shall link to the payment advice raised to finance for goods received under post-receipt payment terms.
FR-001.14: The generation logic for the gate entry number is not yet defined and is recorded as an open item.
FR-001.15: The consignment carrier value set shall be reconciled against the BRD, which defines five values - Transport, Courier, Own Vehicle, Individual (External Person) and Individual (Internal Person). The Courier Type field as currently specified carries four values and shall be aligned so that no value is lost.
 [IMG]
Figure 4.4a - BRD extract shown during the session: the six natures of inward transaction captured at gate entry, the workflow statement establishing the gate entry number as the reference for all subsequent activity and for vendor payment, and the features paragraph listing the details the administrator may configure for capture.
{Heading2} 4.5 Corrections from Session 07 (Part 2)
The Screen 1 field descriptions were reviewed line by line and two of them were found to say something other than what was intended. Both descriptions were analyst-generated, and both have been corrected in the field reference table at section 4.3.
The Name of the Person field was described as capturing the name of the gate personnel or the truck driver. It should capture the truck driver only. The person staffing the gate and the driver delivering the consignment have different roles, and recording either one in the same field loses the distinction.
The Contact Number field was described as enabling notification to the responsible party on arrival. It is the truck driver's number and has no connection to the supplier. Drivers change from delivery to delivery, so the number carries no durable relationship to anything. Where the supplier is to be told that their consignment has arrived, that notification derives from the supplier having been selected on the screen - the supplier is already held in the database with their own contact details - and not from the driver's phone number.
The internal arrival notification triggered by the For Whom / Department Name field is correct as written and is unchanged. Where a consignment is intended for a particular department or individual, that party is notified.
The Courier Type value list was found to contain an erroneous value and has been aligned to the five values defined in the BRD.
Note: these were errors of interpretation rather than of transcription. The descriptions were inferred from the wireframe images, and where a field label was ambiguous the inference generalised it - "responsible party" in place of the specific party actually meant. Analyst-generated blue italic text warrants this kind of line-by-line review precisely because it is inference.
FR-001.16: The Name of the Person field on the gate entry screen shall capture the name of the truck driver only. Gate personnel shall not be recorded in this field.
FR-001.17: The Contact Number field shall capture the contact number of the truck driver. It shall carry no linkage to the supplier record.
FR-001.18: Notification to the supplier that a consignment has arrived shall be derived from the supplier selected on the gate entry screen, using the contact details held against that supplier in the master, and shall not use the truck driver's contact number.
FR-001.19: The internal arrival notification triggered by the For Whom / Department Name field shall be retained as specified. Where the consignment is intended for a named department or individual, that party shall be notified on arrival.
FR-001.20: The Courier Type value list shall carry the five values defined in the BRD - Transport, Courier, Own Vehicle, Individual (External Person) and Individual (Internal Person). The value previously recorded as Self-foot is withdrawn as an error.
 [IMG]
Figure 4.5a - The Screen 1 field reference table as reviewed during the session, with the two descriptions requiring correction highlighted: the conflation of gate personnel with the truck driver, and the generic reference to a responsible party in place of the truck driver.
{Heading2} 4.6 Corrections from Session 07 (Part 3)
The box count captured at gate entry cannot serve as the reference value against which unloading is reconciled. At the gate only a minimal set of details is taken - in practice the name of the person carrying the consignment and a contact number - and the primary document presented at that point is frequently a goods receipt carrying no invoice or box-level breakdown. Where a box count is recorded at all it is a glance at the back of a truck, not a verified figure.
The gate entry stage therefore has two distinct parts. The first is the gate entry proper, where the vehicle is admitted against minimal detail. The second is the capture of consignment details as the vehicle reaches the unloading dock, which is where the document, the invoices and the full box detail are recorded and where the reliable expected count originates.
Note: this does not introduce a new count. The expected value is already defined - No. of Boxes in GEN, captured on Screen 4a and established as the reconciliation baseline by FR-004.2. What Part 3 settles is the standing of the gate entry figure relative to it: indicative, not authoritative. The consequential corrections are made in FR-007.6 and FR-007.8.
FR-001.21: The number of boxes captured at gate entry under FR-001.12 shall be treated as indicative only. It shall not serve as the expected value against which unloading is reconciled; that value is the expected box count recorded at Receipt Details (No. of Boxes in GEN, FR-004.2).
{Heading1} 5. FR-002: Consignment Details &amp; Workflow Configuration - Screen 2
{Heading2} 5.1 Overview
Screen 2 is accessed by the relevant department after gate entry is created. It captures detailed consignment classification, completes any data not entered at Screen 1, and configures which downstream workflow steps apply to this specific consignment.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Not every load goes to the same place. Most of it goes onto the shelves. Some is needed immediately by the workshop, where a vehicle is already on the ramp waiting for the part. Some needs careful handling because it is glass or a windscreen. This tells the system which of those it is, before anyone touches the truck. The system then guides the operator down the right path - it will not offer a shelf location for something going straight to the workshop, and it shows a warning for anything marked fragile. The reason for setting this up front, rather than deciding at the shelf, is that the person unloading at six in the morning is usually not the person who knows why the load was ordered. Marking it at the start means the instruction travels with the load instead of living in somebody head.
Example: A windscreen arrives for a car already on the ramp in bay 2. Before unloading, the load is marked fragile and straight to workshop, with the vehicle number noted against it. When the operator scans the carton the screen shows a handle-with-care warning and tells him to take it to the workshop, not to a rack. He never has to know why, and the car is not held up while somebody works out where the windscreen went.
{Heading2} 5.2 Functional Requirements
FR-002.1: Screen 2 shall be pre-linked to the GEN created on Screen 1. Where Screen 2 is accessed independently, the GEN shall be selected from a dropdown of gate entries already created, and shall not be typed in free text.
FR-002.2: Screen 2 shall allow selection of Product Category and Supplier Category. Selecting a product category shall filter the available supplier list.
FR-002.3: The system shall support a configurable Workflow Applicability Confirmation section. The dealer admin shall define, per product-category and supplier combination, which of four downstream steps are applicable by default.
FR-002.4: The workflow applicability defaults shall appear as pre-checked checkboxes on Screen 2. The receiving user shall be able to override any checkbox for the current consignment.
FR-002.5: Only checked workflow steps shall be activated for the consignment. Unchecked steps shall be skipped and the consignment shall proceed to the next active step.
FR-002.6: Any fields left blank on Screen 1 shall be completable on Screen 2.
FR-002.7: The system shall support manual GEN entry on Screen 2 for consignments processed without a prior Screen 1 gate entry.
FR-002.8: Product and supplier category selections shall drive OEM brand-specific document type options, reflecting variability in documentation formats across brands.
{Heading2} 5.3 Screen Field Reference
Note: The following table describes Screen 2 (Enter Consignment Details) fields as observed in the wireframe. Descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |
| Product Category | Dropdown | Category of goods in consignment. Drives workflow applicability rules and supplier filtering. |
| Supplier Category | Dropdown | Type of supplier: OEM / Manufacturer / Local Vendor / Other. |
| Supplier | Dropdown | Registered supplier from master list, filtered by Supplier Category. |

| Workflow Confirmation - Receipt Confirmation | Checkbox (default: checked) | Activates final receipt confirmation step before ERP/DMS sync. |
[/TABLE]
{Heading2} 5.4 Amendments from Session 07
Two points were added to the consignment details and workflow configuration screen. The first concerns who defines the workflow. The administrator selects the factors on which default workflows are defined - supplier category, nature of the products, consignment carrier and similar - and defines a default workflow for each combination. The user retains the ability to override the workflow defined for a particular scenario before the receipt process begins.
The second concerns the relationship between the supplier and the categories. Where the user enters the supplier name directly, the supplier category can be derived automatically, because each supplier is tagged to a supplier category during supplier creation. The product categories cannot be derived in the same way and are selected manually from a multi-selection control.
The client is a business entity with multiple recipients of consignments, all of whom use the platform. Where the gate entry already holds the supplier and the product category, the administrator defines which person or persons the consignment is routed to.
Note: the asymmetry between supplier category and product category is worth preserving in the screen design. One field fills itself once the supplier is known; the other cannot, because a single supplier may deliver products spanning several categories in the same consignment.
FR-002.9: The administrator shall select the factors on which default workflows for the receipt of consignments are defined, including supplier category, nature of the products and consignment carrier.
FR-002.10: The user shall be able to override the workflow defined for a particular scenario before the receipt process is started.
FR-002.11: Where the user enters the supplier name directly, the supplier category shall be derived automatically from the supplier record, since each supplier is tagged to a supplier category as part of supplier creation.
FR-002.12: Product categories shall be selected manually through a multi-selection control and shall not be derived from the supplier.
FR-002.13: The system shall support multiple recipients of consignments within the client business entity, and the administrator shall define the person or persons to whom a consignment is routed where the gate entry holds the supplier and product category.
FR-002.14: Irrespective of the nature of the consignment, the first step on opening the inward flow shall be entry of the consignment details, comprising product category, supplier category where not entered at gate entry, supplier name, applicable document, gate entry number and courier type.
FR-002.15: Where the gate entry number was not created on this system, the user shall be able to enter the number that was created manually at the gate.
 [IMG]
Figure 5.4a - BRD extract: administrator selection of the factors on which default workflows are defined with user override before start of receipt, the pending gate entry list by workflow stage, and the consignment detail fields entered on Screen 2.
{Heading2} 5.5 Corrections from Session 07 (Part 2)
The requirement that the gate entry number may be manually entered where Screen 2 is accessed independently was queried and corrected. Manual typing of the number is not intended. Once a gate entry has been created on Screen 1 it becomes available for selection, and where the user comes to Screen 2 directly they choose the gate entry from a dropdown of those already created rather than keying the number by hand.
Note: the distinction is between selecting an existing record and creating one by typing its identifier. Free-text entry of a gate entry number invites transcription errors and could attach a consignment to a number that does not exist. The existing provision for entering a number raised manually at the gate - where the gate is not on this system - is a different case and is unaffected.
FR-002.16: Where Screen 2 is accessed independently of Screen 1, the gate entry number shall be selected from a dropdown of gate entries already created. It shall not be entered as free text.
FR-002.17: A gate entry created on Screen 1 shall become available for selection on Screen 2 immediately on creation.
FR-002.18: The provision for entering a gate entry number raised manually at a gate that is not on this system, as specified in FR-002.15, is unaffected by FR-002.16 and remains available.
{Heading1} 6. FR-003: Inward Operations Dashboard - Screen 3
{Heading2} 6.1 Overview
Screen 3 provides real-time visibility into the status of all active consignments across the three primary inward workflow stages: Pending for Unload, Pending for Checking, and Pending for Binning. Used by warehouse supervisors to prioritise daily operations.
{Heading2} Layman Description
One screen showing every load currently in the building and what stage each has reached - arrived at the gate, waiting to unload, being unloaded, being checked, being put away. Alongside each one it shows how long it has been sitting at that stage. The stage on its own is not worth much; almost any system can show a status. What makes this screen worth having is the time. A load at waiting to unload is perfectly normal. A load that has been at waiting to unload for six hours is a problem, and it stays invisible unless the screen puts the number in front of you. This is the screen a supervisor keeps open through the day, and the one a manager looks at to find out where the work is piling up.
Example: At 4 pm the screen lists 6 loads. Five have moved on in the last hour. One has been sitting at waiting to unload since 10 am, because the bay was blocked and nobody escalated it. The supervisor sees the six-hour figure, sends two men across, and it is cleared before closing. Without the ageing column the same load would have been found the next morning, still on the truck, with demurrage running against it.
{Heading2} 6.2 Functional Requirements
FR-003.1: The dashboard shall display three real-time pending queues: Pending for Unload (trucks awaiting unloading), Pending for Checking (GENs awaiting consignment check), and Pending for Binning (GENs awaiting bin assignment).
FR-003.2: Each queue shall display the count of pending items as a summary tile with a drill-down view showing individual consignment records.
FR-003.3: Pending consignment records shall display: Gate Entry Number (GEN), Date, Supplier, and a quantity/value metric relevant to the stage.
FR-003.4: The dashboard shall reflect only the workflow steps that are active for each consignment as configured in Screen 2.
FR-003.5: The dashboard shall update in real time as consignments are processed through each stage.
{Heading2} 6.3 Screen Field Reference
Note: The following table describes Screen 3 (Inward Operations Dashboard) as observed in the wireframe. Descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
[TABLE]
| Field / Column | Control Type | Description |
| Pending for Unload | KPI tile + expandable table | Count of consignments awaiting Truck Unload. Drill-down shows: Gate Entry Number, Date, Supplier, Value. |
| Pending for Checking | KPI tile + expandable table | Count of GENs awaiting Consignment Check. Drill-down shows: Gate Entry Number, Date, Supplier, Number of Boxes. |
| Pending for Binning | KPI tile + expandable table | Count of GENs awaiting Binning. Drill-down shows: Gate Entry Number, Date, Supplier, Number of Units. |
| Gate Entry Number (GEN) | Text / link | The GEN reference number. Clickable to drill into consignment detail. |
| Date | Date display | Date of gate entry creation. |
| Supplier | Text | Supplier name or code as registered in the master. |
| Value / Count | Currency / Number | Estimated consignment value (for Unload queue) or box/unit count (for Checking/Binning queues). |
[/TABLE]
 [IMG]
Figure 6.3a - Screens 1-3 composite: Gate Entry (Screen 1), Consignment Details (Screen 2), and Inward Operations Dashboard (Screen 3) showing three pending work queues
{Heading2} 6.4 Amendments from Session 07
The dashboard as specified reports pending queue counts with drill-downs but has no time dimension. The BRD requires pending activity to be presented in aging buckets, and the reason is practical: a large warehouse may carry a substantial backlog of consignments on which binning has not been completed, and a flat count gives no indication of which of them has been waiting longest.
Note: the buckets are a filter over the existing pending queues rather than a new queue. The counts already on the dashboard should become filterable by age, so the operator can move from what is pending to what is pending and overdue.
FR-003.6: The inward operations dashboard shall present pending activity in aging buckets and shall allow the pending queues to be filtered by age.
FR-003.7: The aging buckets shall be the last 7 days, the last 15 days, the last 30 days and 60 days and over.
FR-003.8: The aging dimension shall apply to the pending queue counts already specified for the dashboard, so that the operator can establish which pending items have been outstanding longest.
FR-003.9: The dashboard shall present the pending gate entries at each workflow stage, with the respective user seeing the number of consignments pending at each stage.
 [IMG]
Figure 6.4a - Wireframe reviewed during the session: gate entry creation on Screen 1, consignment detail capture with the workflow applicability confirmation on Screen 2, and the pending-for-unload, pending-for-checking and pending-for-binning queues on Screen 3 which the aging buckets will filter.
{Heading1} 7. FR-004: Receipt Details - Invoice &amp; Transporter Capture - Screens 4a &amp; 4b
{Heading2} 7.1 Overview
Screens 4a and 4b constitute the Receipt Details stage (Step 2 of the inward process). Screen 4a captures the formal consignment documentation: the primary document reference, total box and invoice counts, and individual invoice-level details. Screen 4b captures transporter and vehicle information and provides an overview of all invoices added, with document upload options. Both screens are accessed via Step 2 after the gate entry (Screen 1) has been created. Data from Screen 1 (GEN, primary document type) is automatically carried forward.
For certain OEM brands where invoice data is available in the back end (e.g., linked to the ship list or GRN reference), invoice details will auto-populate in the invoice summary table without manual entry, based on brand-level configuration.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Invoice Capture (FR-004.3 to .5) - One vehicle very often carries more than one invoice from the same supplier. This lets you enter all of them against that single arrival, instead of forcing you to create a separate arrival for each invoice. As each one is added the screen builds up a list - invoice number, date, amount - with a running total at the bottom, the way a shopping bill adds up as items are scanned. There is usually also a tick against each invoice so you can mark which ones you have physically received, because the papers and the goods do not always travel together. The reason this matters is that one truck is one physical event. If the system makes you split it into three arrivals, then your box counts, your unloading records and your shortage reports are split three ways as well, and nothing reconciles cleanly afterwards.
Example: A truck arrives carrying 3 invoices worth Rs 40,000, Rs 15,000 and Rs 70,000. All three are entered against that one arrival, the screen shows Rs 1,25,000 at the bottom, and the storekeeper ticks two as received because the third invoice copy is missing from the envelope. The 48 boxes on the truck are counted once, against one arrival, not divided arbitrarily between three.
Invoice Capture (FR-004.2; FR-007.15) - Before unloading begins, somebody records how many boxes this load should contain. That number comes off the paperwork - the invoice or the packing list - and it becomes the figure the system checks against. As boxes come off the truck and are scanned, the system counts them and compares. If the count falls short it says so. This sounds obvious, but it is the single thing that decides whether a shortage is ever found at all. Without a number recorded before unloading there is nothing to compare against, and a missing box only comes to light later, when somebody goes looking for that part and cannot find it - by which time the vehicle has gone and the supplier has no reason to accept it. The timing is the whole point: the number has to exist before the boxes come off, not after.  This capability also spans Section 10.
Example: The paperwork says 50 boxes, so the expected count is recorded as 50. The men scan 48 and the system shows 2 short while the driver is still in the yard. The storekeeper walks out, checks the truck, finds one box behind the cabin and scans it, then marks the remaining one short on the driver copy. One box is recovered and one is claimed - both on the same day, with the driver present.
Invoice Capture (FR-004.4) - For some suppliers - usually the large vehicle manufacturers - the invoice already exists in the back-office system before the truck arrives. Where that is the case the details are fetched automatically instead of being typed in again by the storekeeper. He sees the invoice number, date and amount already filled in, and his job becomes checking them against the paper in his hand rather than copying them across. Two things come out of this. It is faster, at a moment when a driver is standing and waiting. And it removes typing mistakes on exactly the fields that cause disputes - an invoice number with two digits transposed will not match at payment time, and somebody will spend an afternoon finding out why. Whether it works usually depends on the supplier, so it is worth asking which suppliers it covers, not simply whether the feature exists.
Example: A consignment arrives from a vehicle manufacturer. Instead of the storekeeper typing invoice number MSIL/24/8891, its date and Rs 2,40,000 by hand, the screen shows them already filled in. He compares them against the printed invoice, sees they match, and moves on in seconds. For a small local supplier the same screen comes up blank and he types the details in as usual.
{Heading2} 7.2 Functional Requirements
FR-004.1: Screen 4a shall carry forward the GEN and GEN Date from the gate entry record. Both fields shall be read-only and pre-populated.
FR-004.2: The system shall capture the total number of boxes in the GEN and the total number of invoices in the GEN. These counts serve as the expected values against which actual unloading is tracked in Screen 6a.
FR-004.3: Screen 4a shall allow the user to capture individual invoice details via the Capture Invoice Details collapsible accordion. Each invoice record shall include: Invoice Number, Invoice Date, and Total Invoice Amount. The system shall allow multiple invoices to be added sequentially using an Add Next control.
FR-004.4: For OEM brands where invoice data is available from the back end (linked to the primary document reference such as ship list or GRN number), the system shall automatically populate the invoice summary table. This behaviour shall be configurable per OEM brand.
FR-004.5: Screen 4b shall display a summary table of all invoices added on Screen 4a (Details of the Invoices Added) showing: Invoice No., Date, Amount, and a Received checkbox per invoice. The table shall show a Total row aggregating all invoice amounts.
FR-004.6: Screen 4b shall capture transporter and driver details: Name of the Transporter (from registered list or free entry), Vehicle Number as per GR Documents, Name of the Driver, and Contact Number.
FR-004.7: The Capture Transporter Details section on Screen 4b shall be collapsible. If transporter details have been pre-captured at gate entry (Screen 1), they shall be pre-filled and editable.
FR-004.8: Screen 4b shall provide document upload options for: GRN photograph and Invoice photographs. Uploaded documents shall be associated with the GEN record.
FR-004.9: If the user attempts to proceed (Next) without entering invoice details, the system shall display an amber confirmation dialog: You have not added details of the invoices. Are you sure? Pressing Next after confirmation shall allow progression without invoice details.
FR-004.10: Submission state shall be communicated via toast notifications: success (Details Added Successfully), failure (Failed! Please Try Again), or incomplete invoice warning (amber dialog).
{Heading2} 7.3 Screen Field Reference - Screen 4a
Note: Screen 4a (Enter Consignment Details - left panel) captures the formal receipt document and invoice-level detail. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.

Note: the summary table renders on Screen 4b, not Screen 4a.  [IMG]
Figure 7.5a - FRD section 7.2 as reviewed in session, with the Screen 4a attribution in FR-004.5 highlighted as the wording to be corrected
{Heading1} 8. FR-005: Truck Unload Initiation &amp; GEN Selection - Screens 5A &amp; 5b
{Heading2} 8.1 Overview
Screens 5A and 5b are the Truck Unload initiation stage (part of Step 3). Screen 5A allows the warehouse operator to identify and load the GEN associated with the truck to be unloaded. This can be done either by directly entering the GEN or by searching using other known details (supplier, document number, date, status). Once the GEN is selected, all associated consignment data is auto-filled and the operator selects which invoices to unload in this session. Screen 5b allows final verification and editing of transporter and driver details before commencing the physical unloading scan.
The WMS operates on handheld terminals (rugged mobile devices, approximately smartphone-sized) used by warehouse staff at the point of physical activity. All scanning, photo capture, and box-level recording is performed on these devices.
{Heading2} Layman Description
To start unloading you tell the system which arrival you are working on, normally by entering its reference number. But the man standing at the truck often does not have that number - the slip is in the office, or the load came in on the previous shift. So the same screen lets him find it using things he can see or ask: the supplier name, the document number on the paperwork, or simply today date. The results come back on the same screen and he picks the right one. The detail worth checking is whether that search sits inside the screen or is a separate one. If finding the load throws him out to another screen and back, he loses his place, and quite often the system then asks him to re-enter details it already holds.
Example: The operator at bay 3 has no slip. He types the supplier name and today date. Two arrivals come back - one with 12 boxes, one with 48. He knows the truck in front of him is the big one, picks the 48, and the screen fills in the supplier, the document and the invoices on its own. He starts scanning within seconds, without walking back to the office to ask anyone.
{Heading2} 8.2 Functional Requirements
FR-005.1: Screen 5A shall allow the warehouse operator to enter or select the GEN associated with the truck to be unloaded. Upon selection, all consignment data associated with that GEN shall be automatically populated on screen.
FR-005.2: If the operator does not know the GEN, Screen 5A shall provide a secondary search panel (Find GEN Using Other Details) that allows lookup by: Supplier Name, Supplier Category, Product Category, Document Number, Document Date, and Status. Search results shall display matching GENs with their document reference, supplier, date, and Pending Docs count.
FR-005.3: Upon GEN selection, Screen 5A shall auto-populate: GEN Date, Supplier Category, Supplier, Product Category, Primary Document Type, Document Number, Document Date, and associated invoices.
FR-005.4: Screen 5A shall allow the operator to select specific invoices within the GEN to unload in the current session. The system shall display the expected box and unit counts for the selected invoices.
FR-005.5: Screen 5b shall display the transporter and driver details pre-filled from Screen 4b. All fields shall be editable to accommodate changes at the time of physical truck arrival (e.g., vehicle number change).
FR-005.6: The Truck / Vehicle No. Reported field on Screen 5b shall support camera capture to photograph the vehicle number plate, providing evidence of the actual vehicle present during unloading.
{Heading2} 8.3 Screen Field Reference - Screen 5A
Note: Screen 5A (Unload Truck - GEN selection view) allows the operator to identify the correct consignment before commencing unloading. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
