{Heading2} 8.5 Amendments from Session 07
Selecting the gate entry and document to unload against currently carries no validation. The BRD requires that a document number which is not eligible for the selected gate entry is rejected outright rather than accepted and reconciled later, because unloading a truck against the wrong document creates confusion that is difficult to unpick once the goods are on the floor.
FR-005.7: Where a document number that is not eligible for the selected gate entry is entered while choosing the gate entry or document to unload against, the system shall not accept it.
FR-005.8: The system shall display a rejection message and shall require the user to enter or select the correct document number before the unload can proceed.
FR-005.9: The document number entered shall be validated as matching the selected gate entry.
{Heading2} 8.6 Corrections from Session 07 (Part 3)
Finding a gate entry by other known details is a search facility within Screen 5A, not a screen in its own right. The operator begins typing in the GEN field, the lookup options appear, the operator searches and selects, and the consignment loads - all without leaving the screen. The board reviewed in session had drawn the lookup as a separate panel reached by leaving Screen 5A, and had additionally drawn a further screen carrying supplier name, receipt date and document capture, none of which belongs to a search.
The list of criteria in section 8.1 - supplier, document number, date, status - is a list of the fields the operator may search on. It is not a list of screens, and was flagged in session as reading that way.
Note: no requirement changes as a result of this. FR-005.2 already specifies the lookup as a secondary search panel on Screen 5A. The correction is to the wireframes, which render it as a separate screen, and the two requirements below state the rule explicitly so the boards can be corrected against it.
FR-005.10: The Find GEN Using Other Details lookup shall be presented within Screen 5A as a search panel or overlay. It shall not be implemented as a separate screen, and the operator shall not be required to navigate away from Screen 5A to use it.
FR-005.11: The lookup shall offer search criteria only. It shall not capture supplier, document or consignment data, all of which are captured at the Receipt Details stage and are auto-populated on Screen 5A once the gate entry is selected.
 [IMG]
Figure 8.6a - the board reviewed in session: the Select GEN lookup drawn as a separate panel over Screen 5a, carrying supplier name, receipt date and invoice capture fields that do not belong to a search
 [IMG]
Figure 8.6b - FRD section 8.1 as reviewed in session, with the comma-separated search criteria highlighted as the passage read as a list of separate screens
{Heading1} 9. FR-006: Box Scanning, Condition Assessment &amp; Variance Check - Screens 6a, 6b &amp; 6c
{Heading2} 9.1 Overview
Screens 6a, 6b, and 6c constitute the core unloading execution stage (Step 3 - Truck Unload). Screen 6a is the primary box scanning screen where each physical box is identified (by QR scan or manual entry), its condition assessed, and a running unloading WIP tracker maintained. Screen 6b provides a search utility to check the unloading status of a specific box or part number. Screen 6c shows the variance between the document-declared quantities and the actual quantities scanned, along with a quality variance summary by condition type.
The unloading stage operates on handheld terminals. Each box has a QR code or barcode on the outer carton. The operator scans each box as it is unloaded from the truck. Box condition is assessed and recorded in the same step. The unloading WIP tracker (at the bottom of Screen 6a) provides a running count: how many boxes have been unloaded, how many remain, and how many are OK vs not OK.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Box Scanning and Condition (FR-006.1 to .4) - Boxes are not counted in a lump. Each one is scanned individually as it comes off the truck, the same way a courier scans parcels at your door. The moment a box is scanned the screen asks a single question - what does this box look like? The operator picks from a short list: fine, packing torn, damaged, wet, crushed. He answers while the box is still in his hands. The timing is the whole point of the feature. If the system waited until the end of unloading, the operator would remember that one box was torn but not which one, and a claim against a specific box could not be raised. Asking at the moment of the scan ties the condition to that particular box permanently. It is also the only moment at which the person who actually saw the box is the one answering the question.
Example: A truck has 48 boxes and box 17 has a torn corner. The operator scans box 17, taps packing torn, and carries straight on to box 18 without stopping. At the end the record shows 47 boxes fine and box 17 torn. The supplier is told box 17 arrived torn and cannot dispute which one it was. Had the question been asked only after all 48 were down, the operator would have known one box was torn but not which - and nothing could have been claimed.
Box Scanning and Condition (FR-006.5; FR-006.28) - A photograph is only demanded when there is something worth photographing. If the operator marks a box as fine, the system does not ask for one and does not slow him down. The moment he marks it as torn, damaged or wet, the camera becomes compulsory and the entry cannot be saved until a photo is taken. The reason for tying it to the condition rather than making it always-on is practical. A man unloading 200 boxes will not photograph every one, and if the system insists he will find a way round it - photographing the floor, or marking everything as fine so it stops asking. Demanding a photograph only for the few boxes that are actually damaged keeps the rule enforceable, and those are the only photographs anyone will ever need. When the supplier disputes the claim, the photograph is what settles it.
Example: Out of 48 boxes, 46 are fine and are scanned through in a couple of minutes with no camera prompt at all. Box 17 is marked packing torn and box 31 wet - for those two the screen will not let the operator continue until he photographs them. Three weeks later the supplier says the boxes left the factory in good order. Two photographs, timestamped and tied to those box numbers, close the argument.
Unloading Status Lookup - A plain lookup that answers one question: has this box or this part come off the truck yet, yes or no. It is used constantly and almost never by the person doing the unloading. Somebody from the workshop needs a part urgently and wants to know whether it has arrived; rather than walking to the bay and interrupting, they type the part number and get an answer. The important word is only. This screen does not let anyone change the condition of a box, or add a photograph, or record damage - it just reports. Keeping it read-only matters, because condition should be recorded once, by the man holding the box, and not later by somebody at a desk who never saw it. The second thing worth checking is whether it still works after the unloading has been closed, since most of the questions come afterwards.
Example: At 11 am the workshop rings: a brake disc is needed for a car on the ramp. The service advisor types the part number and sees unloaded - it came off at 9:50. He sends someone to collect it. The next day he checks a different part and gets not unloaded, so he orders it from another branch instead of waiting. Neither enquiry disturbs the men working the truck.
Unload Session Control - Unloading a large consignment does not always finish in one go. A shift ends, a bay is needed, rain starts. This lets the operator save where he has got to and pick it up later without losing the scans already done, and it records honestly that the load was only partly unloaded rather than quietly treating it as complete. The other half of the feature is what happens at the end. When every box on the list has been scanned, the system offers to close the session - but the operator can decline. That matters more than it sounds. The list is not always right; there is often one more box on the truck that nobody expected. If the system closes automatically the moment the list is satisfied, that extra box has nowhere to go and gets left off the record entirely.
Example: At 6 pm 31 of 48 boxes are down and the shift ends. The operator saves; the next morning another man opens the same session and carries on from box 32. Later, all 48 are scanned and the system asks whether to finish. He can see a further carton still in the truck, so he declines, scans it as box 49, and only then closes - with 49 recorded against a list that said 48.
{Heading2} 9.2 Functional Requirements
FR-006.1: Screen 6a shall allow the warehouse operator to scan or manually enter the box number (Unit / Box No.) for each physical box as it is unloaded from the truck. Each scan creates a record in the unloading tracker linked to the GEN.
FR-006.2: Box numbers shall be scannable via QR code or barcode reader on the handheld device. Manual keyboard entry shall also be supported as a fallback.
FR-006.3: If a scanned box number does not match the expected manifest for the GEN, the system shall display a confirmation dialog (Box No. does not match with the uploaded data. Would you like to upload it?). The operator may confirm to add the box as a discrepancy record, or reject to discard the scan.
FR-006.4: For each box scanned, the operator shall be able to record the box condition via the Update Box Condition accordion. Condition options shall include: OK, Packing Tampered, Damaged, Visible Scratch, Without Packing, Wrong Delivery. Multiple conditions may be selected for a single box.
FR-006.5: When a box condition other than OK is selected, the system shall require photo evidence capture (camera) before the box entry can be saved. An audio/video note capture is planned as a future enhancement.
FR-006.6: The Mark for Open Delivery action shall be available for boxes assessed as damaged, tampered, or wrong delivery. Marking a box for open delivery initiates a formal inspection process (in the presence of the transporter/driver) as per the defined open delivery workflow.
FR-006.7: The system shall provide a real-time Unloading WIP Status bar on Screen 6a showing: Unloaded (boxes scanned), Balance (expected minus scanned), OK (condition OK boxes), and Not OK (boxes with any non-OK condition). The WIP status shall be refreshable via a manual refresh button rather than real-time auto-update, to avoid excessive processing load.
FR-006.8: Screen 6b shall provide a search function (Enter Box No. or Part No.) allowing any user to check the unloading status of a specific box or SKU/part number. The box or part number shall be mandatory. The search shall return whether the item is unloaded or not unloaded; it shall not return variance information. The lookup shall remain available after the unload session has been closed, so that the unloading status of a box can be established retrospectively. This feature is subject to the availability of SKU-level box manifest data from the OEM/supplier.
FR-006.9: Screen 6c shall display a variance summary for the consignment: expected vs actual box counts (As Per Document / Actual / Variance) and a quality variance table (No. of boxes/units by condition: Damage / Tampered Packaging / Scratch / Broken / Wrong Delivery / Total).
FR-006.10: The Finish Truck Unload button on Screen 6a shall commit the unloading session. Any remaining unscanned boxes shall be flagged as Balance in the WIP tracker.
FR-006.11: The system shall allow the unloading session to be saved at any point without completing it. All boxes scanned, conditions recorded, photographs captured and remarks entered up to that point shall be persisted, and the operator shall be able to resume from the same state. This supports shift changes and breaks during a long unload.
FR-006.12: Where a ship list has been uploaded against the GEN, Screen 6a shall display it in a Shipment Details (Shiplist) panel showing the file name and upload timestamp, with a View action to open it and a Replace action to upload a corrected or alternative ship list. Replacing the ship list shall re-evaluate the expected box manifest for the consignment.
FR-006.13: Where a scanned box is not present on the uploaded ship list, the system shall raise a Mismatch with Shiplist exception against that box and shall require the operator to choose one of two actions before continuing: Set Aside, which segregates the box for return to the transporter and excludes it from the received count; or Add Document, which attaches supporting documentation to justify acceptance of the box into the consignment.
FR-006.14: The system shall maintain a Recent Box Entries log on the box condition screen, listing the most recently processed boxes with box number, recorded quality, status indicator and timestamp, together with a View All action to open the full log for the session.
FR-006.15: Screen 6a shall display an Unload Progress indicator showing boxes unloaded against total expected boxes and the corresponding completion percentage, updated in real time as each box is scanned.
FR-006.16: Screen 6c shall display an Unload Completion status and an Exception Summary stating the number of boxes marked as exceptions and confirming that the associated open delivery actions have been enabled and handled.
FR-006.17: Screen 6c shall provide a Print Summary action producing a printable record of the unloading session covering quantity variance, document-wise breakdown, quality variance and the exception summary.
FR-006.18: Unloading shall capture variance at primary box level only. Where a Units column is shown for reference, it shall be derived from document data and not from physical counting. Unit-level verification and the resulting quantity reconciliation shall be performed at the Consignment Check stage (see Section 12), because the transport vehicle cannot be held while every carton is opened.
{Heading2} 9.3 Screen Field Reference - Screen 6a
Note: Screen 6a (Unload Truck - box scanning view) is the primary unloading execution screen operated on handheld terminals. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
[TABLE]
| Field / Section | Control Type | Description |
| Gate Entry Number (GEN) | Dropdown / auto-fill | The GEN associated with this unload session. Auto-filled from Screen 5A selection or selected manually. |
| Supplier | Read-only display | Auto-filled from GEN record. Supplier name for this consignment. |

Mandatory where the recorded condition is anything other than OK; the box entry cannot be saved until the photograph is captured. Not required where the condition is OK. Future enhancement: audio/video note capture also planned. |

Would you like to upload it? with YES / NO options. YES adds the box as a discrepancy record; NO cancels the entry. |

{Heading2} 9.7 Amendments from Session 07
Three points were added to truck unloading, all concerning how an unload session is closed.
The first is the auto-finish confirmation. Where the ship list has been uploaded and every box on it has been scanned, the system should recognise that the unload is complete and offer to finish it rather than waiting for the operator to decide. The confirmation must be declinable: the operator may know that a further box is present which was not on the list, and must be able to refuse the prompt and continue scanning.
The second is partial unloading. A consignment of fifty parts may arrive with only thirty present because the supplier did not hold the remaining stock, with the balance following on a later date. In that situation the operator finishes the unload knowing it is incomplete. The system should state how many cartons remain outstanding, ask for confirmation, and record that the truck was not completely unloaded.
The third is saving and resuming. Where a consignment runs to a large number of cartons, the operator should be able to save the session and return to it rather than being required to complete it in one pass.
Note: the existing Finish Truck Unload button is not replaced by any of this. It remains available at all times; what is added is a system-initiated prompt when the list is satisfied, a declinable path out of that prompt, and an explicit record when the session is closed short.
FR-006.19: Where the ship list has been uploaded and every box on it has been scanned, the system shall trigger a confirmation that the unload appears complete and shall offer to finish the truck unload.
FR-006.20: The auto-finish confirmation shall be declinable. Where the operator observes an additional box that was not on the ship list, the operator shall be able to decline the prompt and continue scanning.
FR-006.21: The Finish Truck Unload action shall remain available to the operator at all times and shall not be conditional on the ship list being satisfied.
FR-006.22: Where Finish Truck Unload is selected while cartons remain outstanding, the system shall state how many cartons are still expected and shall request confirmation before closing the session.
FR-006.23: Where the operator confirms closure with cartons outstanding, the system shall record that the truck was not completely unloaded, so that the incomplete unload is visible subsequently.
FR-006.24: The operator shall be able to save an unload session and resume it later, so that a consignment running to a large number of cartons need not be completed in a single pass.
 [IMG]
Figure 9.7a - Wireframe reviewed during the session: the unload screen with the ship list uploaded and the expected box list, box condition capture with mandatory photograph and the mismatch-with-shiplist alert, and the check variance summary with quantity and quality variance, exception summary and unload completion progress.
{Heading2} 9.8 Corrections from Session 07 (Part 3)
The Check Unloading Status search answers one question and returns one kind of answer: has this box or part come off the truck, yes or no. Its purpose is operational and narrow. A workshop needs a part urgently while a hundred-box consignment is being unloaded; somebody searches the part number and learns whether it is already off the truck. Nothing about box condition is involved.
Screen 6b as drawn carried Update Box Condition, Mark for Open Delivery and Take Photo, and the field reference described the condition panel as reachable without selecting a gate entry. That is the behaviour objected to in session. Box condition is recorded once, on Screen 6a, at the moment the box is scanned during unloading, where the operator is physically holding the box. Offering the same controls from a status lookup creates a second and unwitnessed route to the same record.
Check Variance was similarly misplaced. It is not an immediate step after a status search. Variance is a consignment-level reckoning that becomes meaningful only once every box has been unloaded, every condition recorded and the session closed; running it against a single box mid-unload returns nothing usable.
Note: the rule a developer needs from this is that the status search is read-only. It takes a mandatory box or part number, returns unloaded or not unloaded, and offers no action. Condition is updated on Screen 6a and nowhere else; variance is read on Screen 6c after the unload is finished. The Update Box Condition, Mark for Open Delivery and Take Photo rows have been removed from the section 9.4 field reference accordingly, and FR-006.8 has been corrected in place to drop variance from what the search returns and to state that the lookup survives closure of the session.
FR-006.25: The Check Unloading Status search on Screen 6b shall be read-only. It shall not present Update Box Condition, Mark for Open Delivery, photo capture or any other action that writes to the box record.
FR-006.26: Box condition shall be recorded only on Screen 6a at the point of scanning during unloading. No alternative route to recording or amending box condition shall be offered from a status lookup.
FR-006.27: Check Variance shall not be presented as an action available from the status search. Variance shall become available only once the unload session has been closed, at the consignment level, as specified in FR-006.9 and FR-006.16.
FR-006.28: Photograph capture shall be mandatory where a recorded box condition is anything other than OK, and the box entry shall not be saved until the photograph has been captured. Where the condition is OK no photograph shall be required.
Note: the wireframe control is labelled Take Photo (Mandatory) without qualification. That is the name of the control, not the rule. The rule is conditional on the recorded condition, consistent with FR-006.5, FR-007.5 and FR-008.7.
 [IMG]
Figure 9.8a - the Screen 6a field reference as reviewed in session, with the optional wording on Take Photo highlighted as the description to be corrected
{Heading1} 10. FR-007: Unloading Without Shipment Document - Screens 6d, 6e &amp; 6f
{Heading2} 10.1 Overview
A significant proportion of consignments arrive without a ship list. In this situation the system has no box-level manifest to validate against, so the unloading screens operate in a reduced mode: the expected box list and the ship list comparison are suppressed, and the box count captured at gate entry becomes the only expected reference available.
Two sub-scenarios are handled here. In the first, the outer carton still carries a scannable primary code, or at minimum a legible part number, so each box can be identified individually as it comes off the truck (Screens 6d and 6e). In the second, the dealer has configured a counter-based approach in which the operator simply increments a count for each box unloaded and records exceptions separately (Screen 6f).
Everything downstream of identification is unchanged. Box condition assessment, the open delivery recommendation, mandatory photo capture and remarks all behave exactly as defined in Section 9, so that an exception raised in this mode is handled identically to one raised against a ship-list consignment.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Vehicles turn up with no paperwork more often than anyone would like - the invoice is following by courier, or the driver has lost it. The question is whether the system lets you carry on. If it insists on a document before anything can be unloaded, the truck sits in the yard. This provides ways of unloading without one, chosen by an administrator to suit how the business works: counting the boxes on a counter as they come off, scanning them if they happen to carry codes, or recording them against a reference created on the spot. What it deliberately does not do is invent an expected quantity. With no document there is nothing to compare against, so the system records what physically came off and leaves the shortage question until the paperwork arrives, rather than producing a variance figure that means nothing.
Example: A truck arrives at 7 am with no invoice; the supplier says it will be emailed by noon. Rather than holding the vehicle, the storekeeper unloads under the counter method, tapping the counter once per box as it comes off, and finishes with 48. At 1 pm the invoice arrives showing 50. The two figures are compared then, and 2 boxes are raised as short - with the unloading already done and the truck long gone.
{Heading2} 10.2 Functional Requirements
FR-007.1: Where no shipment document is available against a GEN, the unloading screen shall open in reduced mode and shall display a persistent banner reading Shipment document not available. Ship-list-dependent controls, including the expected box list and the ship list comparison, shall be suppressed.
FR-007.2: The operator shall scan the primary box code printed on the outer carton to identify each box. Where that code is not legible, is damaged, or is absent, the system shall accept manual entry of the part number printed on the carton as an alternative box identifier.
FR-007.3: Unloading shall be recorded box-number-wise. The system shall maintain a running list showing each box number processed, its recorded quality and its status indicator, with the ability to tap any row to update its condition.
FR-007.4: Each box shall be assessed for condition immediately after it is unloaded, using the same condition set defined in FR-006.4: OK, Packing Tampered, Damaged, Visible Scratch, Without Packing and Wrong Delivery.
FR-007.5: Where a box is assessed as anything other than OK, the open delivery recommendation, mandatory photo capture, optional remarks and the Recent Box Entries log defined in Section 9 shall apply without modification. Every exception shall open the same condition flow irrespective of which unload approach is in use.
FR-007.6: The system shall display a Box Unloading summary showing Unloaded, OK and Not OK counts, and a Variance Summary (Box Number Wise) showing boxes unloaded against the expected box count recorded at Receipt Details (No. of Boxes in GEN, FR-004.2) together with the remaining balance. Document-based quantity variance shall not be attempted in this mode; the expected box count recorded at Receipt Details shall serve as the expected value.
FR-007.7: The unload approach shall be configurable by the dealer admin. The selected approach shall be displayed on screen as Configured Unload Approach with a Set by Admin indicator, and the operator shall not be able to change it at the point of unloading.
FR-007.8: Where the counter approach is configured, Screen 6f shall present a Use Counter control which the operator increments once for each box unloaded. The running count shall be displayed prominently and shall be reconcilable against the expected box count recorded at Receipt Details (No. of Boxes in GEN, FR-004.2).
FR-007.9: Under the counter approach, the system shall provide a Box Quality Update section with an Add Not OK Box action. This shall allow the operator to raise an exception against a temporary box reference without scanning, recording the quality and status of that box while the remaining boxes are counted in bulk.
FR-007.10: The system shall not attempt reconciliation of ordered quantity against received quantity at the unloading stage. Where a consignment arrives without a document, the shortfall between what was ordered and what was received shall be determined at the Consignment Check stage, once boxes are opened and contents are counted.
FR-007.11: Finish Unload shall commit the session. All boxes scanned, counted or raised as exceptions shall be carried forward to the Consignment Check stage as the received population for that GEN.
{Heading2} 10.3 Screen Field Reference - Screen 6d
Note: Screen 6d (Unload Truck - no shipment document) is the reduced-mode box scanning screen. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.

| Field / Section | Control Type | Description |

| Gate Entry Number (GEN) | Read-only text | Gate Entry Number carried forward from the gate entry record. Displayed as the master reference for the unloading session. |
| Supplier | Read-only text | Supplier name resolved from the consignment record. |

| Box Unloading (Box Number Wise) | Summary tiles | Three running counters - Unloaded, OK and Not OK - updated as each box is processed. |
| Box list (Box No. / Quality / Status) | Scrollable table | One row per processed box showing the box number, the quality label recorded against it, and a status icon. Helper text reads Tap a box to update condition, allowing an earlier assessment to be corrected. |

| Save | Secondary button | Persists the session without closing it, per FR-006.11. |
| Check Variance | Primary button | Opens the variance view for the session. |

{Heading2} 10.4 Screen Field Reference - Screen 6e
Note: Screen 6e (Update Box Condition - no shipment document) records the condition of an individual scanned box. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
[TABLE]

| Scanned box header | Read-only text | Displays the scanned box number with a Scanned Box sub-label and a box icon. Identifies which box the condition is being recorded against. |
| No shipment details available. Quality update is required for each box. | Static notice | Explains that because there is no manifest to validate against, a condition assessment is mandatory for every box rather than only for exceptions. |
| Box Condition | Radio group (six options) | OK, Packing Tampered, Damaged, Visible Scratch, Without Packing, Wrong Delivery. Single selection per box. |

| Cancel / Save Condition | Button pair | Cancel discards the assessment for this box; Save Condition commits it and returns to the scanning screen. |

{Heading2} 10.5 Screen Field Reference - Screen 6f
Note: Screen 6f (Unload Truck - counter approach) is used where boxes are neither listed on a manifest nor individually identifiable. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
[TABLE]
| Field / Section | Control Type | Description |
| No shipment details and boxes are not scannable. | Static banner | Confirms the conditions that caused the counter approach to be applied. |
| Gate Entry Number (GEN), Supplier | Read-only text | Consignment identification carried forward from the gate entry record. |
| Configured Unload Approach | Read-only panel with Set by Admin tag | Displays the unloading method configured for this scenario by the dealer admin. Not editable at the point of unloading. |
| Use Counter | Numeric display with increment control | Large running box count with a plus control. Helper text reads Press + after each box is unloaded. Serves as the received box count for the session. |
| Box Quality Update | Table with Add Not OK Box action | Allows exceptions to be raised without scanning. Each row carries a temporary box reference, the quality recorded and a status icon. |
| Box Ref. (Temporary) | System-generated reference | Sequential reference issued by the system for an exception box, so that the exception can be tracked even though the box has no supplier identifier. |
| Each exception opens the same condition flow with Condition, Open Delivery, Photo, and Remarks. | Static helper note | Confirms that exception handling is identical across all three unload approaches. |
| Save / Finish Unload | Button pair | Save persists the session; Finish Unload commits it and releases the consignment to the Consignment Check stage. |
[/TABLE]
 [IMG]
Figure 10.5a - Screens 6d, 6e and 6f: reduced-mode unloading with the Shipment document not available banner, box-number-wise unloading list and variance summary (Screen 6d); mandatory per-box condition capture with open delivery, photo and remarks (Screen 6e); admin-configured Use Counter approach with Box Quality Update and Add Not OK Box (Screen 6f).
{Heading2} 10.6 Amendments from Session 07
Where neither the shipment list nor the boxes are scannable, the BRD sets out three distinct options rather than two. The first is to print stickers and scan them back. The second is a tap counter incremented once per box. The third is a plain entry of the total number of boxes unloaded, with no per-box tracking at all - the operator unloads the consignment and records that fifty or sixty boxes were received.
The first two are already specified. The third is not currently represented as a separate configuration, and it is materially different from the other two: it produces a total with no record of the individual boxes, which is the right answer where per-box tracking would cost more than it is worth.
Note: these are three configuration choices, not a sequence. The applicable option should be settled before unloading begins rather than being offered to the operator on the floor.
FR-007.12: Where neither the shipment list nor the boxes are scannable, the system shall support three distinct and separately configurable counting options.
FR-007.13: The third option shall be a plain entry of the total number of boxes unloaded, recorded as a single manual total with no per-box tracking, no scanning and no incrementing.
FR-007.14: The three options - print and scan back stickers, tap counter per box, and plain total count entry - shall each be available as a distinct configuration for a consignment.
{Heading2} 10.7 Corrections from Session 07 (Part 3)
Two requirements in this section reconciled the boxes unloaded against the box count recorded at gate entry. That count is not dependable, for the reasons set out in section 4.6: at the gate the consignment may be accompanied by nothing more than a goods receipt, and the box figure, where taken at all, is indicative. Reconciling a physical unload against it invites a variance that reflects only the weakness of the original count.
Note: the baseline is unchanged from what the document already specifies - the expected box count recorded at Receipt Details on Screen 4a, established by FR-004.2. FR-007.6 and FR-007.8 have been corrected in place to reference it rather than the gate entry count. No new baseline is introduced.
FR-007.15: Reconciliation of boxes unloaded shall be against the expected box count recorded at Receipt Details (No. of Boxes in GEN, FR-004.2). The box count captured at gate entry shall not be used as the reconciliation baseline.
 [IMG]
Figure 10.7a - FRD section 10.2 as reviewed in session, with the gate entry box count clauses in FR-007.6 and FR-007.8 highlighted as the passages to be corrected
{Heading1} 11. FR-008: Box Sticker Generation for Non-Scannable Consignments - Screens 6g &amp; 6h
{Heading2} 11.1 Overview
The most constrained scenario is one in which the consignment arrives with no shipment document and the boxes carry no scannable code at all. Counting alone gives a total but leaves no way to attach a condition, a photograph or a remark to a specific physical box, and no way to trace that box through the remainder of the inward process.
To close that gap the system generates its own box references, prints them as stickers, and has the operator paste one sticker on each box. Once pasted, the boxes become scannable and the standard condition flow resumes unchanged. The references are recorded as system-generated so that downstream processes do not mistake them for supplier manifest numbers.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Plenty of goods arrive with nothing to scan - loose cartons, local suppliers, anything repacked in transit. Without a code on the box there is no way to tell box 12 from box 30, and everything after that becomes guesswork. This lets you print a sticker on the spot and put it on the box, giving it its own code for the rest of its life in the warehouse. Once the sticker is on, that box behaves like any other: it can be scanned when unloading, scanned again when it is checked, and referred to by number in a claim. The practical points to check are whether the printing happens at the bay or only in an office, and whether the code stays tied to that consignment - a sticker that just prints a random number nobody can look up is not much use.
Example: A local supplier delivers 20 plain cartons with no markings at all. The operator prints 20 stickers at the bay and puts one on each carton as it comes off. Carton 6 is crushed, so it is scanned and marked damaged. When the claim goes in, the supplier is told carton 6 - a box that had no identity at all twenty minutes earlier and could otherwise only have been described as one of the twenty.
{Heading2} 11.2 Functional Requirements
FR-008.1: Where neither a shipment document nor a scannable box code is available, the system shall support a sticker-based unload approach. The approach shall be selectable by the dealer admin and shall be displayed on screen as Print Box Stickers &amp; Scan under Configured Unload Approach, with helper text reading Use printed stickers to unload and track boxes.
FR-008.2: The system shall accept a Total Boxes to Print value. It shall default to the box count recorded at gate entry and shall remain editable by the operator to accommodate a discrepancy discovered at the dock.
FR-008.3: The system shall generate a unique temporary box reference for each box to be printed, in the format BX-nnnn, sequential within the GEN.
FR-008.4: The system shall render a Sticker Preview of the generated references. Each sticker shall carry the box reference as human-readable text, a QR code and a linear barcode, so that it can be read by either scanner type in use at the dealership.
FR-008.5: The system shall print the full set of stickers in a single action, labelled with the count to be printed. On-screen guidance shall instruct the operator to print and paste one sticker on each unloaded box.
FR-008.6: Once a sticker has been pasted, the box shall be scanned back into the system. The condition screen shall confirm the scan with a Printed Sticker Scanned indicator and shall prompt the operator to update quality before saving.
FR-008.7: Box condition assessment, the open delivery recommendation, mandatory photo capture, optional remarks and the Recent Box Entries log shall behave identically to FR-006 and FR-007. The sticker approach shall change only how a box is identified, never how an exception is handled.
FR-008.8: The system shall flag each temporary box reference as system-generated rather than supplier-issued, so that reconciliation, returns and any subsequent claim against the supplier do not treat it as a manifest reference.
FR-008.9: The system shall record that box ownership is unverified in this scenario. Where there is neither a ship list nor a supplier code, the system cannot establish that a given box belongs to this dealer rather than to another consignee sharing the vehicle. Ownership shall be confirmed at the Consignment Check stage, when the box is opened and its contents identified.
{Heading2} 11.3 Screen Field Reference - Screen 6g
Note: Screen 6g (Print Box Stickers) generates and prints temporary box references. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
[TABLE]
| Field / Section | Control Type | Description |
| No shipment details and boxes are not scannable. | Static banner | Confirms the conditions that caused the sticker approach to be applied. |
| Gate Entry Number (GEN), Supplier | Read-only text | Consignment identification carried forward from the gate entry record. |
| Configured Unload Approach - Print Box Stickers &amp; Scan | Read-only panel with Set by Admin tag | Displays the sticker approach as configured by the dealer admin, with helper text explaining that printed stickers will be used to unload and track boxes. |
| Total Boxes to Print | Numeric field with generate control | Number of stickers to produce. Defaults to the gate entry box count and remains editable. |
| Sticker Preview | Preview grid | Shows the generated stickers before printing. Each tile carries the box reference in text, a QR code and a linear barcode. |
| Print and paste one sticker on each unloaded box. | Static helper note | Operating instruction shown beneath the preview grid. |
| Back | Secondary button | Returns to the previous screen without printing. |
| Print n Stickers | Primary button | Sends the full sticker set to the connected label printer in a single action. The button label reflects the count entered. |
[/TABLE]
{Heading2} 11.4 Screen Field Reference - Screen 6h
Note: Screen 6h (Update Box Condition - printed sticker) records the condition of a box identified by a system-generated sticker. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
[TABLE]

| Scanned box header | Read-only text | Displays the temporary box reference with a Printed Sticker Scanned sub-label, distinguishing it from a supplier-issued box number. |
| Update quality before saving. | Static notice | Reminds the operator that a condition assessment is required for every box under this approach. |
| Box Condition | Radio group (six options) | OK, Packing Tampered, Damaged, Visible Scratch, Without Packing, Wrong Delivery. Single selection per box. |

| Cancel / Save Condition | Button pair | Cancel discards the assessment; Save Condition commits it and returns to scanning. |
[/TABLE]
 [IMG]
Figure 11.4a - Screens 6g and 6h: Print Box Stickers with Total Boxes to Print, sticker preview showing box reference, QR code and barcode, and the Print n Stickers action (Screen 6g); condition capture against a scanned printed sticker (Screen 6h).
{Heading1} 12. FR-009: Consignment Check Initiation - Screen 7a
{Heading2} 12.1 Overview
Consignment Check is the second physical stage of the inward process. It begins once the truck has been unloaded and has left the premises, at which point the dealer holds a set of primary boxes whose outer condition has been assessed but whose contents have not been verified. This is the stage at which boxes are opened, secondary packages are scanned and the quantity actually received is established.
Not every consignment requires it. Where the primary box and the secondary box are the same physical unit - a bumper, a windshield glass, a bonnet, a fender, a door or a tail door typically arrives as a single packed item - there is nothing to open and no inner verification to perform. Such consignments are routed straight to binning. Screen 7a is therefore a routing decision point as much as a data capture screen.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Once the truck is empty, there is a decision to make about how thorough to be. Either every carton is opened and checked against the paperwork, item by item, or the goods go straight onto the shelves and are checked later or not at all. Both are legitimate: a sealed consignment from a trusted manufacturer does not need opening, while a load from a supplier with a history of short deliveries does. What matters is that the choice is made deliberately, recorded, and not reversible halfway through - because the two routes produce very different records, and mixing them means nobody can later say whether a discrepancy was missed or never looked for. The system should remember which route was taken, so that when a shortage surfaces two weeks on, you know whether anyone actually checked.
Example: Two loads arrive on the same morning. The manufacturer consignment goes direct to bin - sealed cartons, straight to the racks, no opening. The second, from a supplier who short-shipped twice last quarter, is put through a full check and two items are found missing from a carton. A month later, when a part from the first load cannot be found, the record shows it went direct to bin and was never opened - so the search starts on the shelves rather than with the supplier.
{Heading2} 12.2 Functional Requirements
FR-009.1: Screen 7a shall allow an unloaded consignment to be retrieved by GEN, either by selecting from a list of consignments awaiting checking or by entering the GEN directly. GEN Date shall auto-populate on selection.
FR-009.2: Supplier Category, Supplier and Product Category shall auto-populate from the consignment record established at gate entry and Screen 2. These fields shall be read-only at this stage.
FR-009.3: The user shall select or enter the Document Type, Document Number and Document Date applicable to the consignment being checked. Document Type shall be driven by the OEM-brand-specific configuration described in Section 3.4.
FR-009.4: The screen shall present two mutually exclusive routes: Consignment Check, which opens the full secondary verification flow with box opening and item-level scanning; and Direct to BIN, which bypasses secondary verification and proceeds directly to product identification and binning.
FR-009.5: Direct to BIN shall be applicable where the primary and secondary box are the same physical unit, so that no opening or inner verification is possible or required. In this case the consignment proceeds straight towards billing and storage.
FR-009.6: The route selected shall be recorded against the consignment for audit purposes. Where a dealer applies a uniform policy for a given product or supplier category, the route shall be defaultable by configuration, consistent with the workflow applicability mechanism defined in FR-002.3.
FR-009.7: Cancel shall exit the screen without committing a route selection, leaving the consignment in the pending-for-checking queue.
{Heading2} 12.3 Screen Field Reference - Screen 7a
Note: Screen 7a (Consignment Check) initiates the post-unload checking stage and selects the route the consignment will follow. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |
| GEN | Search / select field with lookup control | Gate Entry Number of the consignment to be checked. The adjacent lookup control opens a picker listing consignments that have completed unloading and are awaiting check. |
| GEN Date | Read-only text | Date of the selected gate entry. Auto-populated on GEN selection. |
| Supplier Category | Read-only text | Supplier classification carried forward from the consignment record. |
| Supplier | Read-only text | Supplier name carried forward from the consignment record. |
| Product Category | Read-only text | Product classification carried forward from the consignment record. Together with Supplier Category it determines the document types available below. |
| Document Type | Dropdown | Type of document accompanying the consignment - for example ship list, invoice, GRN or delivery challan. Options are driven by OEM brand configuration. |
| Document Number | Dropdown with free entry | Reference number of the selected document. Where document data is available in the back end the number may be selected; otherwise it is entered manually. |
| Document Date | Date field (DD/MM/YYYY) | Date printed on the accompanying document. |
| Consignment Check | Primary action button | Routes the consignment into the full secondary check flow, in which primary boxes are opened and their contents verified. |
| Direct to BIN | Primary action button | Routes the consignment directly to product identification and binning, bypassing secondary verification. Applied where primary and secondary box are the same unit. |
| Cancel | Secondary button | Exits without committing a route selection. |
[/TABLE]
 [IMG]
Figure 12.3a - Screen 7a (Consignment Check): GEN and GEN Date, auto-populated supplier and product classification, document type, number and date, and the two routing actions Consignment Check and Direct to BIN.
{Heading1} 13. FR-010: Direct to Bin - Secondary Scan &amp; Product Identification - Screens 8a &amp; 8b
{Heading2} 13.1 Overview
Screens 8a and 8b handle the identification of an individual part once the routing decision has been taken. The operator scans the unit or box, the system resolves it against the product master, and the part's storage destination is displayed before any physical movement occurs.
Two operational realities shape this design. The first is that a part may be needed immediately by a vehicle already in the workshop, in which case it must be segregated rather than binned. The second is that warehouse staff routinely tear off the primary packing to save space - a packed bonnet occupies roughly one and a half times the space of an unpacked one - and the MRP identifier is attached to that packing. Once the packing is removed the part loses its identity, and staff resort to writing the part number on the item by hand. The system therefore prints a replacement identification sticker that can be attached directly to the part.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Product Identification - Scanning a part should immediately tell the operator everything he needs to deal with it, without him going to look anything up. The description confirms he has the right item - part numbers are long and similar, and the description is how a human checks. The storage location tells him where it goes, which is the next thing he will need. And a fragile indicator warns him before he handles it carelessly. The value is in getting all of it from one scan. If the operator has to check a printed list for the location, or ask somebody whether an item needs careful handling, the system is only doing part of the job and the rest depends on the individual - which is exactly what goes wrong when the experienced man is on leave.
Example: The operator scans a part and the screen shows Front Brake Disc, location B-12-04, not fragile. He walks straight to B-12-04. The next scan comes up as a headlamp assembly with a fragile marker, so he carries it separately rather than stacking it. Neither decision needed anyone else, and a man on his second day makes the same choices as a man with ten years.
Disposition Routing - A part that has just been scanned does not always belong on a shelf. Four things can sensibly happen to it, and the system offers all four at that moment. It can be issued straight for use. It can go to the workshop for a specific vehicle, with the vehicle number recorded so it can be found later. It can be put away on a shelf now. Or it can be set aside to be put away later, when there is time. Offering the choice at the point of scanning is what makes it work, because that is when the operator has the part in his hand and knows what is happening to it. If the decision has to be made later on a different screen, the part gets put on a shelf by default and someone has to go and fetch it again an hour afterwards.
Example: A brake pad set is scanned. The workshop has been waiting for it, so the operator picks deliver to workshop and enters vehicle MH-01-2233. The part never goes to a rack. The next scan is a filter with no urgency, so he picks proceed to bin and puts it away. The third is a slow-moving item at the far end of the warehouse, so he picks bin later and carries on unloading rather than walking the length of the building for one part.
Bin Later Queue - Work on a bay is constantly interrupted. An operator scans a part, then something else arrives and he scans that too before finishing with the first. Rather than losing the first part or forcing him to deal with it before he can move on, the system puts it into a pending list automatically. He carries on, and comes back to the list when there is a gap. This matters because the alternative is worse than it sounds. If the system insists each part is finished before the next can be scanned, the operator starts working round it - putting parts aside physically and scanning them later from memory, which is where errors come in. A queue that fills by itself matches how the work actually happens rather than how it is supposed to happen.  This capability also spans Section 14.
Example: The operator scans a radiator, then a pallet arrives at the bay and he scans four more parts while helping unload it. The radiator has gone into the pending list on its own. Twenty minutes later he opens the list, sees five items waiting, and works through them one at a time - putting each away properly. Nothing was left on the floor unrecorded and nothing had to be remembered.
{Heading2} 13.2 Functional Requirements
FR-010.1: Screen 8a shall carry forward the GEN, Supplier and Document Number from Screen 7a. These fields shall be read-only, presented under a collapsible Consignment Details section.
FR-010.2: The Initiate Direct Binning section shall accept the Unit / Box No. by QR or barcode scan, or by selection from a list of boxes received against the consignment. A delete control shall allow an incorrectly scanned box to be cleared without leaving the screen.
FR-010.3: On successful scan, the system shall retrieve and display from the product master: Product Code, Product Description, MOQ, Binning Zone and Binning Location. The operator shall therefore know the destination of the part before picking it up.
FR-010.4: Where the product master defines the part as fragile, the FR indicator adjacent to Product Code shall be activated and visually highlighted. Where the part is not fragile the indicator shall remain in a neutral state. This gives the operator an immediate handling signal for items such as headlights and windshield glass.
FR-010.5: The system shall provide print controls against the product identification and the binning destination, producing the stickers defined in Section 13.5. This allows a replacement identifier to be attached directly to a part whose primary packing has been removed.
FR-010.6: Screen 8b shall provide a Deliver to Workshop section capturing the Vehicle Number, for parts routed directly to a vehicle under repair rather than to storage.
FR-010.7: Where the scanned part matches an open workshop demand held in the system, the IForU (Issue for Usage) action shall be activated automatically. This signals to the operator that the part is required by a vehicle currently in the workshop and must be segregated rather than binned.
FR-010.8: The operator shall select one of four dispositions for each scanned part: IForU, which segregates it for immediate issue to the workshop; Proceed to BIN, which opens the binning flow described in Section 14; BIN LATER, which defers binning; or Cancel.
FR-010.9: BIN LATER shall be applied by default where the operator scans a further part without having actioned the previous one. Parts so deferred shall accumulate into a bin-later queue, allowing a trolley load - typically three to four doors, or several windshield glasses - to be carried and binned in a single trip.
FR-010.10: Screen 8b shall provide an Update Product Condition section and an Update Box Condition checklist offering OK, Packing Tampered, Damaged, Visible Scratch, Without Packing and Wrong Delivery, together with a Take Photo action for evidence capture. Condition recorded here relates to the secondary unit and is distinct from the primary box condition recorded during unloading.
FR-010.11: The system shall print two distinct sticker formats as defined in Section 13.5: a D2B sticker for parts going to storage and a D2W sticker for parts going to the workshop.
{Heading2} 13.3 Screen Field Reference - Screen 8a
Note: Screen 8a (Direct to BIN - product identification) resolves a scanned unit against the product master and displays its storage destination. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.

 [IMG]

{Heading1} 14. FR-011: Bin Placement &amp; Location Confirmation - Screen 8c
{Heading2} 14.1 Overview
Screen 8c completes the inward journey by placing the part physically into stock. Two conditions must hold simultaneously for the placement to be correct: the operator must be at the location the system expects, and the part in hand must be the part that belongs there. The screen therefore verifies location and part independently before accepting a quantity.
The design accommodates warehouses where not every bin carries a scannable code, and where space constraints regularly force an operator to place stock somewhere other than the allocated position. Rather than blocking the operator, the system raises a wrong-location alert and records the deviation.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Bin Placement (FR-011.1 to .4; FR-011.13) - Putting a part away requires two scans, not one: the shelf and the part. Scanning the part alone proves the right item was picked up but not where it went. Scanning the shelf alone proves somebody stood at the shelf. Only both together prove that this part went into this location, and that is the record the whole stock system depends on afterwards. It sounds like extra work but it takes a couple of seconds and it prevents the single most expensive mistake in a warehouse, which is stock recorded in a place it is not actually in. Once that happens the part is effectively lost: the system says it exists, somebody goes to fetch it, it is not there, and a replacement is ordered while the original sits three shelves away.
Example: The operator carries a filter to B-12-04. He scans the shelf label, then scans the filter. Only then does the system accept the put-away. On another occasion he scans B-12-04 and then a similar-looking filter with a different part number - the screen stops him and shows the mismatch. Without the two scans that part would have been recorded in B-12-04 and lost the day somebody went to get it.
Bin Placement (FR-011.5 to .8) - Alongside the two scans, the operator records how many pieces he is actually putting into that location. Two ways of doing it are needed, because two situations exist. If there are five items and they are individually barcoded, scanning each one is the most reliable. If there are two hundred small clips, scanning each is absurd and typing the number is the only sensible answer. A system that insists on one method for everything will be worked around. The other half is the check: it should not be possible to record putting away more than was picked up. Without that limit, a mistyped quantity creates stock that does not exist, and the error only surfaces at the next stock count when it is far too late to work out where it came from.
Example: The operator picked up 5 filters and puts 5 into B-12-04, scanning each one. Next he has 200 clips and types 200 rather than scanning them individually. Later he mistypes 50 against a line where only 5 were picked up, and the system refuses it and shows the 5. Fifty phantom clips never enter the stock figures.
Bin Placement (FR-011.9; FR-011.14; FR-011.16) - A part is often permitted in more than one place - a main location and one or two alternates, or a fast-moving item stocked in two zones. The system shows the operator which places are allowed for the part in his hand, rather than leaving him to remember or to ask. The more important half is what happens when he scans somewhere else entirely. Warehouses are busy and shelves look alike; scanning the shelf below the right one is an easy mistake. If the system accepts it silently, the part is lost. If it warns that this location is not one of the permitted ones, the operator either corrects himself or makes a deliberate decision to override - and either way somebody knows about it.
Example: A filter is allowed in B-12-04 and B-12-05; the screen shows both. The operator walks to the rack and scans B-13-04 by mistake, one row over. The screen warns that this is not a permitted location for this part. He looks up, sees he is at the wrong rack, moves one row and scans again. The whole correction takes ten seconds and no stock is misplaced.
Bin Capacity - Whether the system knows how much space a location has and how much of it is currently occupied. If it does, it can avoid sending anyone to a shelf that has no room, and the whole business of arriving at a full location and picking an alternative largely disappears. If it does not, every full shelf is discovered by a person standing in front of it. This is worth establishing carefully, because a system may well show a capacity field without anything using it - the field is filled in at setup, never maintained, and the recommendation ignores it. Ask whether it actually prevents a recommendation, not whether the number can be stored.  This capability also spans Section 42.
Example: The system knows C-04-02 holds 40 units and currently has 38. An operator arrives with 5 filters; the system does not send him to C-04-02 at all, because it knows only 2 will fit. He goes straight to C-04-05. Where capacity is stored but unused, he would be sent to C-04-02, discover the problem himself, and go through the exception process instead.
Geolocation - Using the location of the device to confirm the operator is physically at the shelf he says he is at, rather than relying on the barcode scan alone. It closes a specific loophole: an operator can scan a printed sheet of location barcodes from the comfort of the bay and record putaways that never happened. In a warehouse where that is a real risk it is valuable. In most it is over-engineering, and indoor positioning is unreliable enough that false rejections would cost more than the problem is worth. Realistically this is a large-site capability, and worth asking about mainly to establish how sophisticated a competitor is rather than because it is likely to be needed.
Example: In a large multi-floor warehouse the device confirms the operator is on the second floor in aisle C when he scans C-04-02. In a single-room dealership store the same feature would reject valid scans whenever the signal drifted, and would be switched off within a fortnight.
{Heading2} 14.2 Functional Requirements
FR-011.1: On Proceed to BIN, the system shall present the binning screen carrying the Binning Zone and Binning Location already determined at Screen 8a, so that the operator knows where to go before leaving the scanning point.
FR-011.2: SCAN Location shall read the QR code or barcode fixed at the bin location, confirming that the operator has physically reached the correct place.
FR-011.3: Where a bin location carries no scannable code, the operator shall be able to assert arrival using Confirm Location. The system shall record that the location was confirmed manually rather than scanned.
FR-011.4: SCAN Part shall read the identifier of the part being placed, so that part and location are verified against each other and not merely in sequence.
FR-011.5: The Quantity control shall record how many units of the part are being placed at the location, with decrement and increment controls and a default of one.
FR-011.6: The system shall not permit the quantity binned at a location to exceed the quantity picked for binning. This check shall apply across all placements made from a single bin-later batch.
FR-011.7: The system shall support two methods of recording multiple identical parts: scanning each unit individually, or scanning once and entering a count. Which method applies shall be configurable at dealer level.
FR-011.8: Where a part is not scannable - for example where the packing has been removed and the part number written on the item by hand - the system shall accept manual entry of the part number in place of a scan.
FR-011.9: Where the scanned location does not correspond to the allocated location, the system shall raise a Wrong Location alert and shall offer two responses: proceed with binning at the scanned location, recording the deviation against the part; or abandon the placement and relocate to the defined location.
FR-011.10: PICK NEXT to Bin shall advance to the next part in the bin-later queue without leaving the binning flow. Finish shall close the binning session. Cancel shall abandon the current placement.
FR-011.11: Future scope - bin capacity awareness. The system is intended to hold the dimensions, volume and permissible weight of each bin in configuration, so that it can advise whether a target bin can physically accept the item and can distinguish a genuine wrong location from a full one. Weight limits matter because heavy items cannot be stacked above light ones without risk of toppling. This capability is outside Phase 1 scope and is expected to form part of a later bin management release.
FR-011.12: Future scope - geolocation confirmation. Where a dealer operates fixed, surveyed bin locations, the geolocation of the handheld device may be captured at the moment of confirmation to verify placement automatically without requiring a scan. This capability is outside Phase 1 scope.
FR-011.13: Session 04 revision - superseding FR-011.3. The Confirm Location action shall be replaced by a Select Location control presenting a dropdown of the bin locations permitted for the part, comprising the recommended location and any alternative locations. The operator selects the location actually used rather than merely asserting arrival. This preserves the wrong-location check, because a selected location can still be compared against the recommended one, whereas a bare confirmation could not be.
FR-011.14: Where a part has more than one permitted bin location, the system shall display the recommended location together with the other allowed locations, and the operator shall choose between them. This accommodates the common situation in which the recommended bin is already full and the part must be placed elsewhere.
FR-011.15: Future scope - occupancy-aware recommendation. Once bin volume and dimension data is held in configuration (see FR-011.11), the system is intended to indicate the occupancy of a target bin and propose an alternative where it is substantially full. This is envisaged as a chargeable additional service rather than base functionality, and is outside Phase 1 scope.
FR-011.16: The system shall present a deliberately small set of options at the point of binning. Pickers operating the handheld may have limited literacy, and an excess of choices at the moment of placement increases error rather than reducing it. Where the system can determine the correct answer it shall do so rather than asking the operator to decide.
{Heading2} 14.3 Screen Field Reference - Screen 8c
Note: Screen 8c (bin placement) verifies location and part before committing stock to a storage position. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
[TABLE]
| Field / Section | Control Type | Description |
| Deliver to Workshop - Vehicle Number | Text field with print control | Carried forward from Screen 8b. Remains visible so that a part diverted to the workshop mid-flow can still be captured against a vehicle. |
| Update Product Condition | Collapsible section | Carried forward from Screen 8b, allowing a condition observed while handling the part to be recorded before placement. |
| IForU / Proceed to BIN | Action buttons | Retained from the previous screen so that a part can still be diverted to the workshop after the binning flow has been opened. |
| SCAN Location | Scan control | Reads the QR or barcode fixed at the bin location, confirming the operator has reached the allocated position. |
| SCAN Part | Scan control | Reads the part identifier being placed. Accepts manual entry of the part number where the item is not scannable. |
| Confirm Location | Button | Manual assertion of arrival, used where the bin location carries no scannable code. The manual confirmation is recorded as such. |
| Quantity | Numeric stepper | Number of units being placed at this location, with decrement and increment controls. Defaults to one and cannot exceed the quantity picked for binning. |
| Cancel | Secondary button | Abandons the current placement without committing. |
| PICK NEXT to Bin | Primary button | Commits the current placement and advances to the next part in the bin-later queue. |
| Finish | Primary button | Commits the current placement and closes the binning session. |
| Wrong Location ! - Would you like to proceed with Binning | Alert panel | Raised when the scanned location does not match the allocated location. Offers YES, which proceeds at the scanned location and records the deviation, and Place at the defined location, which abandons the placement and directs the operator to the allocated position. |
[/TABLE]
 [IMG]
Figure 14.3a - Screen 8c (bin placement): SCAN Location and SCAN Part controls, Confirm Location fallback, quantity stepper, PICK NEXT to Bin and Finish actions, and the Wrong Location alert offering YES or Place at the defined location.
{Heading1} 15. FR-012: Direct Binning Case 1A - Primary and Secondary Cartons Are the Same - Screens 8A, 8B, 8C &amp; 8D
{Heading2} 15.1 Overview
Case 1A is the most favourable direct binning scenario. Three conditions hold together: both the primary carton and the secondary unit carry scannable codes, the supplier has uploaded its data, and the linkage between primary carton and product is available. The system therefore knows before the carton is opened which part it contains and how many units should be inside.
This section elaborates the flow already defined in FR-010 and FR-011 for that specific condition, and adds two behaviours not previously specified: a unit-by-unit product inspection in which each individual unit receives its own condition and outcome, and a wrong-location override with an explicit bin-later hand-off (Screen 8D).
Case 1A applies only where a carton holds a single SKU packed as identical units - a front bonnet assembly, for example, where the outer carton and the item inside are effectively the same thing. A carton holding several different SKUs falls outside this flow and is handled as described in Section 16.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Case 1A - Same Carton - This covers the simplest kind of goods, where the box you receive and the item inside are effectively the same thing - one carton, one part number - and the carton carries a barcode. There is no opening, no counting of different items inside, no relationship to work out. The consignment is scanned and put away. Even here the system should still require both the shelf and the part to be confirmed at the rack, because the simplicity of the goods does not make misplacing them any less likely. It is worth establishing whether a competitor handles only this straightforward case well and struggles with the more awkward ones, since this is the case that gets demonstrated in sales presentations and the others are where the real difficulty lies.
Example: Fifty cartons of engine oil arrive, one product code, barcode on every carton. The operator scans a carton, the system shows the product and its location, he carries it to the rack, scans the shelf, scans the carton again and it is done. Around fifteen seconds per carton with no opening and no counting - and the same two-scan confirmation as everything else.
Piece Inspection - Where a box has been opened because something is wrong with it, the pieces inside are looked at one at a time rather than judged as a batch. The system walks through them and keeps three running counts: how many were good, how many were damaged, and how many are going onto an insurance claim. The three are kept separate because they go to three different places afterwards. Good pieces go on the shelf and become sellable stock. Damaged pieces do not, and must not be counted as though they were. Claim pieces need to be held aside and photographed rather than put away or thrown out. A single count of pieces received, with no breakdown, means somebody has to sort it out physically later from a pile with no labels on it.  This capability also spans Section 17.
Example: A carton of ten headlamp units arrives crushed at one corner. Opened piece by piece: six are perfect, three are cracked, one is smashed beyond any question. The six go to the shelf as normal stock, the three cracked are set aside for the supplier to decide on, and the smashed one goes straight onto the insurance claim with a photograph. Three outcomes from one carton, each recorded separately.
Location Override - The system suggests a location, but the operator is the one standing in the warehouse and sometimes the suggestion is wrong - the shelf is full, blocked, or the part is bulky and will not fit. He is allowed to put it somewhere else, but the system asks him to confirm rather than letting it happen silently, and then records where it actually went. That last part is what makes the feature worthwhile. An override with no record is the same as losing the stock: the system believes it is in one place and it is in another. An override that is confirmed and recorded means the stock figure stays true, and the pattern of overrides tells you something useful - if one location is overridden every week, its allocation is probably wrong.
Example: The system sends the operator to B-12-04 but the shelf is packed with a delivery from yesterday. He selects a different location, B-12-09, and the system asks him to confirm the change before accepting it. The stock record now says B-12-09, which is where the part actually is. Three weeks later the supervisor sees B-12-04 has been overridden eleven times and reallocates the part properly.
Insurance Routing - A damaged carton is not all bad. Most of what is inside is usually fine, and the point of opening it is to separate the two. Good pieces go onto the shelf and become normal sellable stock. Damaged pieces must not - they go to a set-aside area and onto a claim. The system has to keep those two paths genuinely separate, because the temptation is to treat the carton as a single unit and either shelve all of it or write off all of it. Shelving damaged pieces means a customer eventually receives one, which costs far more than the part. Writing off good pieces means throwing away stock you have paid for. The value of the feature is entirely in the splitting.  This capability also spans Section 21.
Example: A carton of twelve alternators arrives wet; nine are fine and three are water damaged. The nine are binned normally and appear in stock that afternoon, available to sell. The three go to the set-aside rack against a claim, and the system will not let them be put on a shelf. One carton, two destinations, and neither nine good units written off nor three bad ones sold.
{Heading2} 15.2 Functional Requirements
FR-012.1: Case 1A shall be applied where all three preconditions hold: both cartons are scannable, supplier details have been uploaded, and the primary-secondary linkage is available. Where any precondition fails, the system shall fall through to Case 1B.
FR-012.2: The system shall support an optional flag indicating that the primary and secondary carton are the same physical unit for a given item. The flag shall be optional rather than mandatory, and shall serve to identify in advance which items follow this flow. Where a ship list and full configuration are available the linkage is derived automatically; the flag exists for consignments where it is not.
FR-012.3: The Direct to BIN screen shall present two tabs - Current Check and Open Delivery - allowing the operator to switch between the consignment currently being processed and the open delivery queue. The Open Delivery tab shall carry a count badge, and open deliveries shall be viewable and configurable from it.
FR-012.4: Entering the GEN shall auto-populate the consignment details: GEN, Supplier and Document Number.
FR-012.5: Scanning the primary Unit / Box No. shall retrieve and display Product Code, Product Description, MOQ, Binning Zone and Binning Location, together with the fragile (FR) indicator defined in FR-010.4.
FR-012.6: The resolved Product Code shall be overwritable, either by scanning again or through predictive search, so that a misread or mismatched code can be corrected without abandoning the carton.
FR-012.7: Only one product code shall be permitted inside a primary carton under Case 1A. Where different products are found, the operator shall be prevented from proceeding and the carton shall be routed to the check-carton process described in FR-013.13.
FR-012.8: The D2W Vehicle Number field shall appear when a vehicle number is required against an open order, and shall offer sticker printing for the workshop route.
FR-012.9: Where a carton contains more than one unit, the system shall present a Product Inspection - Unit by Unit section requiring a condition and an outcome to be recorded against each unit individually, identified as Unit n of m. Condition shall be OK or Damaged; outcome shall be Proceed to BIN or Set Aside (Insurance). Photographic evidence shall be capturable per unit.
FR-012.10: A unit recorded as damaged shall be routed to Set Aside (Insurance) rather than to a bin, so that an insurance claim can subsequently be raised against the internal part. Good units within the same carton shall continue to binning unaffected. This means a single carton may split between binning and insurance.
FR-012.11: The system shall display running inspection totals for the carton: Good Units, Damaged Units and Insurance Claim counts.
FR-012.12: Binning Action (Screen 8C) shall enforce a two-step verification. Step 1 shall scan the location and confirm Location Verified; Step 2 shall scan the product and confirm Product Verified. Neither step shall be skippable.
FR-012.13: On successful verification the system shall display a Binning Verified Successfully confirmation stating how many good units will be binned and how many units have been moved to Set Aside / Insurance, so that the operator sees the split before committing.
FR-012.14: Confirm BIN shall commit the placement. Cancel Carton shall abandon the carton and reverse the actions recorded against it. The verification sequence - user scans location, user scans product, system confirms binning at the selected location - shall be recorded.
FR-012.15: Where the scanned location differs from the recommended location, Screen 8D shall display both the Recommended Location and the Scanned Location, warn that a wrong location has been selected, and ask whether the operator still wishes to bin at the scanned location. Two responses shall be offered: Cancel and Confirm Override.
FR-012.16: If the override is confirmed, the chosen location shall be recorded against the item as the actual storage location. If cancelled, all actions for that carton shall be cancelled. This shall be stated on screen so the operator understands the consequence before choosing.
FR-012.17: Where a carton is marked for deferred binning, Screen 8D shall confirm the carton reference as marked Bin Later, clear the carton details, present the next-scan control (scan next primary carton or product code), and display a Bin Later Queue with the count of pending cartons.
FR-012.18: Deferred binning shall be completed through the Binning module rather than through the inward flow. The system shall state this on screen so the operator knows where the carton has gone.
FR-012.19: Where the operator scans a second item without having actioned the first, the system shall assume bin-later for the first item, consistent with FR-010.9.
FR-012.20: D2B and D2W stickers shall be printable from this flow in the formats defined in Section 13.5, populated with the live values resolved for the carton.
{Heading2} 15.3 Screen Field Reference - Screen 8A
Note: Screen 8A (Direct to BIN - Case 1A) identifies the carton and resolves it against the product master. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
