{Heading1} 16. FR-013: Direct Binning Case 1B - Single SKU in Primary Carton, Primary-Product Linkage Unavailable - Screens 9a, 9b, 9A, 9B &amp; 9C
{Heading2} 16.1 Overview
Case 1B covers the situation in which the supplier has uploaded both primary carton data and product data, but not the linkage between the two. The system knows the carton exists and knows the part exists, but cannot say which part is inside which carton, or how many units should be there. Expected quantity is therefore unavailable and must be established by physically scanning or counting what comes out of the carton.
The flow runs in three steps: scan the primary carton, scan the product on the secondary unit, then verify product and location and take an action. Because identification depends entirely on the product scan, the carton may hold only one SKU. Finding a second product code inside the same carton invalidates the assumption the flow rests on, and the carton is diverted to the check-carton process.
Screens 9a and 9b give the handheld layout for this case, with separate scan controls for the primary box number and the product code. Screens 9A, 9B and 9C set out the same flow as a three-step sequence with the full field detail, rules and exception alerts.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Sometimes the supplier tells you a carton is on its way but not what is inside it. The carton can be received and scanned, but the system has no expected quantity to check against, because nobody has been told one. What separates a good system here is honesty. It should say plainly on screen that the expected quantity is not known and why - rather than showing a blank that looks like a zero, or worse, quietly treating the number that was found as the number that was expected, which makes every consignment appear to balance perfectly. A warehouse that cannot tell the difference between checked and correct and never checked at all will find out the difference eventually, usually during a stock count.
Example: A carton arrives with a barcode but no contents information. The operator scans it, opens it, and finds 12 pieces. The screen shows expected: not provided by supplier, found: 12. The consignment is not reported as balanced - it is reported as unverifiable, with the reason. When the invoice later shows 15, the 3 missing pieces are still a live question rather than something the system already closed off.
{Heading2} 16.2 Functional Requirements
FR-013.1: Case 1B shall be applied where the supplier has uploaded primary carton data and product data but the linkage between primary carton and product is unavailable. The system shall display the linkage status explicitly as LINKAGE UNAVAILABLE against the carton.
FR-013.2: The flow shall run in the sequence: scan primary carton, scan product on the secondary unit, verify product and location, take action. The sequence shall be shown on screen so the operator knows how many steps remain.
FR-013.3: Screen 9A shall display the primary carton record: Primary Carton No., GEN / Consignment, Supplier, Document Reference, Capture Status, Carton Type, Uploaded By Supplier and Linkage Status.
FR-013.4: Screen 9A shall state that the expected product and quantity will be determined only after product verification, and that the carton may contain only one SKU. The operator shall proceed via a Scan Product (Secondary) action.
FR-013.5: Screen 9B shall retrieve verified product information on scanning the product code from the unit or its secondary packing: Product Code, Product Description, MOQ, Binning Zone, Recommended Location, Alternative Locations, D2W Vehicle No. and IForU Eligible.
FR-013.6: Screen 9B shall present Supplier Quantity as not available, annotated with the reason (no linkage), rather than leaving the field blank. Quantity Received / Count shall be captured through a stepper control in units.
FR-013.7: The system shall state on screen that expected quantity is unavailable because the carton-product linkage is unknown, and that quantity will be captured from the actual scan or count. The absence of a variance figure in this mode is therefore explained rather than silently omitted.
FR-013.8: Scan Another Unit (Same Product) shall increment the Quantity Received under the same primary carton. It shall not move the carton to Bin Later automatically.
FR-013.9: Where a different product code is scanned within the same primary carton, the carton shall be treated as a multiple-SKU case: direct binning shall be stopped and the carton routed to the Check Carton process.
FR-013.10: Scan Next Carton (New Primary Carton) shall be permitted at any point. Where the operator scans the next primary carton without selecting an action for the current one, the system shall automatically mark the current carton as Bin Later.
FR-013.11: Screen 9C shall capture two conditions separately: Primary Carton Condition and Unit / Product Condition for each unit. Each shall support photographic evidence capture.
FR-013.12: Screen 9C shall capture two additional checks as explicit yes/no decisions: Insurance Required and IForU Eligible.
FR-013.13: Screen 9C shall offer four next actions: Proceed to Bin (bin now), Bin Later, IForU (issue for use), and Cancel. The permitted actions shall reflect whether direct binning is allowed for the carton.
FR-013.14: The system shall indicate throughout whether direct binning is allowed or not allowed for the carton in its current state, using a consistent visual key.
FR-013.15: All conditions, quantities and actions shall be captured against User ID, date and time to provide a full audit trail. Actions taken on a carton diverted to Check Carton shall be logged in the same way.
FR-013.16: The system shall raise four distinct on-screen alerts, each with its own sticker or pop-up treatment: Match, where the product is verified successfully and the operator may proceed; Mismatch, where the scanned product does not match supplier data and must be verified before proceeding; Multiple SKU, where more than one product code is detected in the carton and direct binning is not allowed; and No Location, where no bin location is mapped for the SKU and a location must be identified before binning.
FR-013.17: The system shall print a carton sticker carrying the carton reference, SKU, product description, location and GEN. The sticker shall be printed and affixed to the carton or pack after verification, so that a carton identified only by scan retains that identification physically.
FR-013.18: Screens 9a and 9b shall provide the handheld layout for this case, presenting separate scan controls for Primary Box Number and Product Code. The two identifiers are captured independently because, in the absence of linkage, neither can be derived from the other.
{Heading2} 16.3 Screen Field Reference - Screens 9a &amp; 9b
Note: Screens 9a and 9b are the handheld layout for Case 1B, distinguished from Case 1A by the separate primary and product scan controls. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
[TABLE]
| Field / Section | Control Type | Description |
| Direct to BIN / Open Delivery | Tab pair | Switches between the current check and the open delivery queue, as on Screen 8A. |
| Consignment Details - GEN, Supplier, Document Number | Collapsible read-only section | Consignment identification carried forward from the consignment check. |
| Primary Box Number | Scan field with dropdown and delete control | First of two independent scan controls. Captures the outer carton identifier. |
| Product Code | Scan field with dropdown and delete control | Second independent scan control under Initiate Direct Binning. Captures the product on the secondary unit. Separate from the primary scan because no linkage exists between them. |
| Product Description / MOQ | Read-only text pair | Resolved from the product master once the product code has been scanned. |
| Binning Zone | Read-only text with print control | Zone allocated to the part, with sticker printing. |
| Binning Location | Read-only text | Bin position allocated to the part. |
| Deliver to Workshop - Vehicle Number | Text field with print control | Vehicle registration where the part is routed to the workshop, with D2W sticker printing. |
| Update Product Condition | Collapsible section | Condition of the secondary unit, distinct from the carton condition. |
| IForU / Proceed to BIN / Cancel / BIN LATER | Action buttons | The four dispositions available for the scanned part. |
| Update Box Condition | Checklist (six options) | OK, Packing Tampered, Damaged, Visible Scratch, Without Packing, Wrong Delivery. |
| Take Photo | Camera capture | Evidence of the recorded condition. |
[/TABLE]
 [IMG]
Figure 16.3a - Screens 9a and 9b: the Case 1B handheld layout, with separate scan controls for Primary Box Number and Product Code reflecting the absence of carton-product linkage, and the disposition, box condition and sticker print controls carried over from Screens 8a and 8b.
{Heading2} 16.4 Screen Field Reference - Screen 9A (Scan Primary Carton)

| Capture Status | Status badge | How far identification has progressed, for example PRIMARY CAPTURED - the carton is known but its contents are not. |
| Carton Type | Read-only text | Classification of the carton, for example Primary Carton. |
| Uploaded By Supplier | Read-only text | States what supplier data is held, for example primary and product data, making clear that data exists but linkage does not. |

| SCAN PRODUCT (SECONDARY) | Primary action button | Advances to step 2. |
| Note: This carton can contain only one SKU. | Constraint note | States the assumption the flow depends on, so the multiple-SKU exception is not a surprise. |

{Heading2} 16.5 Screen Field Reference - Screen 9B (Scan Product / Secondary Unit)
Note: Screen 9B is step 2 and identifies the SKU and the quantity actually present. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |
| Step 2: Scan product code on the unit / secondary packing | Scan instruction bar | States the action required at this step. |
| Product Code | Read-only text (verified) | SKU resolved from the product scan. Grouped under a Product Information (Verified) heading to distinguish confirmed data from assumed data. |
| Product Description | Read-only text | Descriptive name of the identified part. |
| MOQ | Read-only text | Minimum order quantity for the part. |

| IForU Eligible | Status badge | Whether the part qualifies for immediate issue to the workshop. |

| SCAN NEXT CARTON (New Primary Carton) | Secondary action button | Moves to the next carton. Marks the current carton Bin Later if no action was taken on it. |

{Heading2} 16.6 Screen Field Reference - Screen 9C (Condition Check &amp; Routing)

| Field | Control Type | Description |
| Step 3: Check conditions and choose action | Instruction bar | States the action required at this step. |
| Primary Carton Condition | Dropdown with photo capture | Condition of the outer carton, with photographic evidence. |
| Unit / Product Condition (Each Unit) | Dropdown with photo capture | Condition of the product itself, recorded per unit and independent of the carton condition. |
| Insurance Required | Yes / No selector | Explicit decision on whether the unit is to be set aside for an insurance claim. |
| IForU Eligible | Yes / No selector | Explicit decision on whether the unit is to be issued to the workshop rather than binned. |

| BIN LATER (Bin Later) | Action button | Defers the carton to the bin-later queue. |
| IFORU (Issue for Use) | Action button | Segregates the part for immediate workshop issue. |
| CANCEL (Cancel) | Action button | Abandons the actions recorded against the carton. |
| If multiple product codes are found in this carton, Direct Binning will be stopped and carton will be moved to Check Carton process. All actions are audit logged. | Informational panel | States the exception rule and confirms that the audit trail applies to exception paths as well as normal ones. |
[/TABLE]
{Heading2} 16.7 Case 1B Rules, Alerts and Carton Sticker
Note: The rules and alerts below are as set out on the session wireframe board. Field descriptions in blue italic are analyst-generated.
[TABLE]
| Rule / Alert | Trigger | System Behaviour |
| Same Product - Scan More Units | The same product code is scanned again within the same primary carton | Increments the Quantity Received under that carton. The carton is not moved to Bin Later automatically. |
| Different Product Scanned | A different product code is scanned within the same primary carton | The carton is treated as a multiple-SKU case. Direct binning stops and the carton is routed to Check Carton. |
| Next Carton Scanned | The operator scans the next primary carton without selecting an action for the current one | The current carton is automatically marked Bin Later so that no carton is left in an undetermined state. |
| Match Sticker | Scanned product matches supplier data | Confirms the product is verified, showing SKU, location and quantity, and allows the operator to proceed. A sticker is produced for the carton. |
| Mismatch Sticker | Scanned product does not match supplier data | Warns that the scanned product does not match, and requires the operator to verify before proceeding. |
| Multiple SKU Alert | More than one product code detected in the carton | States that direct binning is not allowed and directs the carton to the Check Carton process. |
| No Location Alert | No bin location is mapped for the scanned SKU | States that no location is mapped and directs the operator to the no-location process to identify a location before binning. |
| Carton Sticker (Print) | Verification completed | Prints a sticker carrying carton reference, SKU, product description, binning location and GEN, to be affixed to the carton or pack after verification. |
[/TABLE]
 [IMG]
Figure 16.7a - Case 1B board (Screens 9A, 9B and 9C): primary carton details with LINKAGE UNAVAILABLE status (Screen 9A); verified product information with recommended and alternative locations, and manual quantity capture in place of an expected figure (Screen 9B); primary carton and per-unit condition capture, insurance and IForU checks, and the four routing actions (Screen 9C); with the Case 1B rules, the match, mismatch, multiple-SKU and no-location alerts, and the carton print sticker.
{Heading1} 17. FR-014: Direct Binning Case 1C - Primary and Secondary Cartons Are the Same, Only the Secondary Product Is Scannable - Screens 10A, 10B, 10C &amp; 10D
{Heading2} 17.1 Overview
Case 1C applies where the primary carton and the secondary carton are the same physical unit, but only the secondary product carries a scannable code. The secondary product details have been uploaded and the primary-secondary linkage is not available. The outer box carries no scannable marking at all, so the carton itself cannot be identified by scanning.
The same consignment header data used in the other direct binning cases - gate entry number, supplier details and document details - is captured on entry to the flow. From there the operator has two routes. A box reference sticker can be printed and stuck on the primary box, after which the box becomes scannable and the flow behaves exactly as Case 1B. Alternatively the operator can continue without the primary sticker, in which case the primary box scan field is hidden and the secondary product is scanned directly.
Scanning the secondary product returns the product code, description, quantity per pack, binning zone, binning location and any alternate locations from master data, together with the Issue for Usage flag where the part is reserved against a vehicle or job. Inspection is then performed unit by unit, damaged units are set aside for insurance, and binning is confirmed through a two-step scan of location and product.
Note: Screens 10A to 10D form the Case 1C board. Screen 10A resolves how the primary carton will be referenced, Screen 10B identifies the SKU from the secondary code, Screen 10C carries out the condition and unit inspection, and Screen 10D performs the verified bin placement. The three sticker formats shown alongside the board are those already defined in section 13.5.
{Heading2} Layman Description
The outer box has nothing to scan but the part inside does. The answer is to give the outer box an identity by printing a label for it, so that from then on it can be scanned like anything else. Being able to see the label on screen before it prints matters more than it sounds: labels are printed in bulk on a busy bay, and discovering after forty of them that the part number was wrong means peeling forty labels off forty boxes. A preview costs a second and prevents that. Whether labelling the outer box is compulsory or optional is also worth asking, since some warehouses only want it where the box will be stored intact and not where it is opened immediately.
Example: Twenty unmarked outer boxes arrive, each holding barcoded parts. The operator selects the first, sees the label on screen showing the part and the box reference, confirms and prints. He sticks it on, scans it, then scans the parts inside. Box 7 turns out to be damaged - and because it now carries a reference, the damage is recorded against box 7 specifically rather than against the consignment as a whole.
{Heading2} 17.2 Functional Requirements
FR-014.1: Case 1C shall be applied where the primary and secondary cartons are the same physical unit, only the secondary product is scannable, secondary product details have been uploaded and the primary-secondary linkage is not available. The system shall state these conditions on the screen header.
FR-014.2: Screen 10A shall capture the same consignment header data used in the other direct binning cases - gate entry number, supplier details and document number - and shall state explicitly that the primary box itself is not scannable.
FR-014.3: Screen 10A shall offer two mutually exclusive routes: Print Box Reference Sticker, to be affixed to the primary box, and Continue Without Primary Sticker, which hides the primary box scan field and takes the operator directly to secondary product scanning.
FR-014.4: Where Print Box Reference Sticker is selected, the printed sticker shall carry the box reference number, the gate entry number and the supplier. Once the sticker is affixed the carton becomes scannable and the flow shall thereafter behave as Case 1B.
FR-014.5: Screen 10A shall display a preview of the box reference sticker before the print command is issued.
FR-014.6: Screen 10B shall provide secondary product scanning by barcode or QR code, with camera capture, and shall provide a manual or predictive search alternative where the printed code cannot be read.
FR-014.7: On a successful secondary scan the system shall retrieve and display the product code, product description, quantity per pack, binning zone, binning location, alternate locations and the workshop vehicle number. All of these values shall be sourced from master data uploaded from the back end - the product master and the location master - and not from transaction data.
FR-014.8: The field currently labelled MOQ shall be understood and relabelled as quantity per pack size. It states how many units are contained in one sale pack and is master data, not an order quantity.
FR-014.9: Products reserved for a specific vehicle or repair order shall be highlighted for Issue for Usage, and the workshop vehicle number shall be shown against the product.
FR-014.10: Primary Box Reference shall be an optional field on Screen 10B. Where a sticker was printed the reference shall be captured; where the operator continued without a sticker the field shall remain empty and the flow shall proceed on the secondary scan alone.
FR-014.11: Box condition shall default to OK and shall be presented in a collapsed panel. The operator shall only expand the panel and change the condition where a problem is physically observed, since the great majority of products are received in good condition. This behaviour applies wherever condition is captured and is stated as a cross-cutting rule in FR-016.
FR-014.12: Screen 10C shall present product inspection unit by unit for the quantity held in the carton, with a per-unit condition, per-unit photograph capture and a per-unit outcome of either Proceed to BIN or Set Aside (Insurance).
FR-014.13: Where a unit is recorded as damaged the operator shall be able to take a photograph of it, and the unit shall be set aside so that an insurance claim can be raised against it.
FR-014.14: Screen 10C shall maintain running counters for Good Units, Damaged Units and Insurance Claim, and shall allow the product and location detail - including the damaged item list - to be printed.
FR-014.15: Screen 10D shall perform binning in two verified steps: Step 1 scan the location, Step 2 scan the product. Each step shall be confirmed on screen before Confirm BIN is enabled, and the system shall state how many units will be binned and how many are set aside.
FR-014.16: Where no binning location exists for the product the system shall raise a No Location Alert offering Enter Location or Notify Admin, governed by role-based access control. Where the operator is not permitted to enter a location a notification shall be sent to the administrator, who shall either grant the privilege to that user or decide the location themselves. This alert shall be raised wherever a specific mapped location has not been defined.
FR-014.17: Where cartons are processed continuously one after another, deferred cartons shall be placed in the Bin Later queue, and the queue with its pending count shall be reachable from Screen 10D.
FR-014.18: Stickers shall be printable from this flow for the primary box reference, for direct-to-bin parts and for direct-to-workshop parts, in the formats already defined in section 13.5.
{Heading2} 17.3 Screen Field Reference - Screen 10A (Primary Box Reference Option)
Note: Screen 10A is the entry point for Case 1C. It confirms the consignment header and forces a decision on how the primary carton will be referenced, since the carton itself carries no scannable code.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |
| Current Check / Open Delivery | Tab bar with count badge | Switches between the consignment currently being checked and the open delivery queue; the badge shows how many cartons are pending open delivery inspection. |
| Consignment Details | Collapsible section | Groups the header data carried in from gate entry so that it can be collapsed once the operator has confirmed it. |
| GEN | Read-only field | Gate entry number of the consignment being checked. |
| Supplier | Read-only field | Supplier name resolved from the gate entry number. |
| Document Number | Read-only field | Shipment document reference captured at receipt. |
| Primary box not scannable notice | Information banner | States that the primary box itself carries no scannable code and directs the operator to choose one of the two options below. |
| 1) Print Box Reference Sticker | Option card with chevron | Prints a system-generated reference sticker to be stuck on the primary box; the note beneath states that once printed the flow follows Case 1B. |
| 2) Continue Without Primary Sticker | Option card with chevron | Hides the primary box scan field and takes the operator straight to secondary product scanning. |
| Preview: Box Reference Sticker | Preview panel | Shows the sticker exactly as it will print - barcode, Box Ref No., GEN and Supplier - before the print command is issued. |
| Print Box Reference Sticker | Action button | Sends the previewed sticker to the label printer. |
[/TABLE]
{Heading2} 17.4 Screen Field Reference - Screen 10B (Scan Secondary Product)
Note: Screen 10B identifies the SKU from the secondary product code. Every field below the scan is populated from the product and location masters, not from the consignment document.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |
| Primary box not scannable notice | Information banner | Repeats the case condition and instructs the operator to scan the secondary product code in order to identify the SKU. |
| Scan Secondary Product | Scan field with camera control | Captures the barcode or QR code printed on the secondary product. |
| Manual / Predictive Search | Alternate action | Used where the code cannot be read; searches the product master by code, description or part number. |
| Product Details (Confirmed) | Section header with status tick | Confirms that the scanned code has resolved successfully against the product master. |

| Binning Location | Read-only field | Recommended bin location from the location master. |
| Alternate Locations | Read-only field | Further permitted bin locations for the same product. |
| D2W Vehicle No. | Read-only field | Vehicle registration where the part is reserved for a workshop job rather than for stock. |
| Primary Box Ref (Optional) | Editable field | Carries the printed box reference where one was produced; remains blank where the operator continued without a sticker. |
| Sticker path note | Information banner | States that where a box reference sticker was printed, this path behaves as Case 1B from that point onwards. |
[/TABLE]
{Heading2} 17.5 Screen Field Reference - Screen 10C (Review Product &amp; Action)
Note: Screen 10C carries out the carton condition check and then the unit-by-unit product inspection, and determines whether each unit proceeds to binning or is set aside.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |
| Product Summary | Read-only panel | Consolidates product code, description, quantity per pack, binning zone, recommended location and alternate locations for confirmation before inspection begins. |
| Update Box Condition (Primary Carton) | Collapsible checklist | OK, Packing Tampered, Damaged, Visible Scratch, Without Packing and Wrong Delivery. Defaults to OK and remains collapsed unless a problem is observed. |
| Product Inspection - Unit by Unit | Repeating block | One block per unit in the pack, headed Unit n of N, so that each physical unit is accounted for individually. |
| Condition (per unit) | Status control | Records the condition of the individual unit as OK or Damaged. |
| Take Photo (per unit) | Camera control | Captures photographic evidence against the individual unit. |
| Outcome (per unit) | Derived status | Proceed to BIN for good units, Set Aside (Insurance) for damaged units. |
| Insurance Claim | Tag | Marks the unit as forming part of an insurance claim. |
| Good Units / Damaged Units / Insurance Claim | Counter tiles | Running totals across the units inspected in this carton. |
| Print Product / Location Details | Action with printer icon | Prints the product and location detail slip, including the damaged item list used to support the claim. |
| iForU | Action button | Routes the product to Issue for Usage where it is reserved against a job. |
| Proceed to BIN | Primary action | Sends the good units forward to the binning step. |
| BIN LATER | Secondary action | Adds the carton to the bin later queue for binning in a later pass. |
| Cancel | Action | Abandons the current carton without recording an outcome. |
[/TABLE]
{Heading2} 17.6 Screen Field Reference - Screen 10D (Binning Action)
Note: Screen 10D performs the verified bin placement in two steps and handles the case where no bin location has been mapped for the product.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |
| Product panel | Read-only summary | Product code, quantity per pack, binning zone, recommended location and other allowed locations for the carton being binned. |
| Step 1: Scan Location | Scan field with verification tag | The bin location is scanned and confirmed with a Location Verified indicator and a success message. |
| Step 2: Scan Product | Scan field with verification tag | The product is scanned and confirmed with a Product Verified indicator and a success message. |
| Binning verified successfully | Confirmation panel | States how many units will be binned and how many are set aside for insurance. |
| Confirm BIN | Primary action | Commits the binning transaction for the verified units. |
| No Location Alert | Warning panel | Raised where no bin location exists for the product; offers Enter Location where role-based access allows, otherwise Notify Admin. |
| Enter Location | Action button | Allows a permitted user to key the bin location manually. |
| Notify Admin | Action button | Sends the location request to the administrator, who either grants the privilege to the user or assigns the location directly. |
| Bin Later Queue | Navigation row with count | Opens the queue of cartons deferred for later binning and shows the number pending. |
| Sticker print panels | Print panels | Three sticker formats are available alongside this screen - primary carton box reference, D2B part and D2W part - in the formats defined in section 13.5. |
[/TABLE]

{Heading1} 18. FR-015: Direct Binning Case 1D - Primary and Secondary Cartons Are the Same, Neither Scannable and No Supplier Data - Screens 10E, 10F, 10G &amp; 10H
{Heading2} 18.1 Overview
Case 1D applies where the primary and secondary cartons are the same physical unit and nothing at all is scannable - neither the primary carton nor the product inside it. No supplier details have been uploaded and the primary-secondary linkage is not available. Since there is no code to read, identification is entirely manual.
On selecting this case the system disables every scan control on the screen. The operator identifies the product through manual or predictive search on product code, description or part number, and then physically counts the contents and keys the total quantity in the carton. Only one product code is permitted inside one primary carton in this case; where a second product is found the operator cannot proceed on this path.
Everything downstream of identification behaves as in Case 1C. Condition capture defaults to OK, damaged units are photographed and set aside for insurance, and binning is confirmed against the recommended location held in master data. Where no location has been mapped, or where the carton is deferred, the exception panel presents the two resulting states.
Note: the Case 1D board labels its four panels by title only and does not carry screen numbers. For reference within this document they are designated Screens 10E to 10H - Identify Product Manually, Review Product &amp; Action, Binning Action and Exception / Next State. This designation is analyst-assigned and is subject to the screen numbering reconciliation recorded as OI-020.
{Heading2} Layman Description
When nothing at all can be scanned, the system should recognise it and adapt rather than leaving the operator fighting a screen designed for barcodes. Three things make the difference. The scanning controls switch off, so nobody wastes time pointing a scanner at an unmarked carton. Finding the part works by typing a few letters, with matches appearing as you type - the alternative is scrolling a list of several thousand parts on a handheld, which nobody will do. And the quantity is typed in, because there is nothing to scan and count. Handled well, an unlabelled consignment takes a few minutes longer. Handled badly, the operator gives up on the system and writes it on paper, which is where the record disappears.
Example: Forty brackets arrive loose in plain cartons. The scanner greys out on its own. The operator types brac, sees Bracket - Front Bumper LH in the list, taps it, and types 40. Two are bent so he records those separately. The whole consignment is on the system in about four minutes, without a barcode and without a paper note that someone has to key in later.
{Heading2} 18.2 Functional Requirements
FR-015.1: Case 1D shall be applied where the primary and secondary cartons are the same physical unit, neither the primary carton nor the product is scannable, no supplier details are available and the primary-secondary linkage is not available. The system shall state these conditions on the screen header.
FR-015.2: On selection of Case 1D the system shall automatically disable all scanning controls, including the primary box scan field and the product code scan field, and shall indicate on screen that scan options are inactive for this case.
FR-015.3: The system shall provide manual and predictive product search, allowing the operator to identify the product by product code, product description or part number where no scannable code exists.
FR-015.4: The operator shall enter the total quantity in the carton manually, having counted the contents physically. The system shall provide a stepper control with a unit label for this entry.
FR-015.5: Only one product code shall be permitted inside one primary carton in Case 1D. Where multiple products are found in the carton the operator shall not be able to proceed on this path and shall be routed to the exception handling.
FR-015.6: Box condition shall be captured against the standard condition set and shall default to OK, in line with FR-016.
FR-015.7: Where a unit is found damaged the operator shall photograph it and set it aside for insurance, exactly as in Case 1C.
FR-015.8: The review screen shall display the manually entered quantity alongside the pack size, the binning zone, the recommended binning location, the alternate locations and the workshop vehicle number, all drawn from master data.
FR-015.9: Binning shall run as a two-step confirmation. Step 1 shall allow the recommended location to be selected as well as scanned, since scanning is unavailable in this case. Step 2 shall confirm the product. Confirm BIN shall only be enabled once both steps report success.
FR-015.10: Where no mapped location exists for the selected product the system shall raise the No Location Alert, offering Enter Location where role-based access allows and Notify Admin otherwise, as defined in FR-014.16.
FR-015.11: The system shall present an exception and next-state panel covering two states: the No Location Alert state, and the Bin Later state which confirms that the carton has been marked for later binning, that product and location details have been cleared, and that the next product in the carton may now be identified.
FR-015.12: The Bin Later queue shall be reachable from this panel with its pending carton count, and the system shall state that binning of deferred cartons is completed through the Binning module.
FR-015.13: All remaining behaviour - Issue for Usage routing, Proceed to BIN, BIN LATER, condition capture and sticker printing - shall be identical to Case 1C and shall not be re-specified for this case.
{Heading2} 18.3 Screen Field Reference - Screen 10E (Identify Product Manually)
Note: Screen 10E is the Case 1D entry point. Its defining characteristic is that the scan controls are present but disabled, so the operator can see why manual identification is being required rather than finding the controls simply absent.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |
| Consignment Details | Collapsible section | Gate entry number, supplier and document number for the consignment being checked. |
| Supplier | Read-only field | Displays that supplier details are not available, which is one of the defining conditions of this case. |
| Neither Primary Carton nor Product Code is Scannable | Option card with chevron | States the case condition and takes the operator into manual or predictive identification. |
| Primary Box (Scan Inactive) | Disabled field | Greyed out and inoperable; the primary carton cannot be scanned in this case. |
| Product Code (Scan Inactive) | Disabled field | Greyed out and inoperable; the product code cannot be scanned in this case. |
| Search Product (Manual / Predictive Search) | Search field with filter control | Searches the product master by product code, description or part number. |
| Product Code / Description result | Result card with confirmation tick | Confirms the product selected from the master before quantity is entered. |
| Enter / count total quantity in carton | Stepper with unit label | The operator counts the contents physically and keys the total; there is no scan-derived count in this case. |
| Scan options inactive in Case 1D | Information note | Confirms to the operator that all scan controls remain disabled for this case. |
| Confirm Product | Primary action | Commits the manually identified product and the counted quantity. |
| Single product code rule | Warning banner | States that only one product code is allowed inside one primary carton and that the operator cannot proceed where multiple products are found. |
[/TABLE]
{Heading2} 18.4 Screen Field Reference - Screen 10F (Review Product &amp; Action)
Note: Screen 10F mirrors Screen 10C, with the quantity shown as manually entered rather than scan-derived.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |
| Product summary | Read-only panel | Product code, description, manual quantity against pack size, binning zone, recommended binning location, alternate locations and workshop vehicle number. |
| Update Box Condition (Primary Carton) | Collapsible checklist | The standard condition set - OK, Packing Tampered, Damaged, Visible Scratch, Without Packing, Wrong Delivery - defaulting to OK. |
| Product Inspection - Unit by Unit | Repeating block | Per-unit condition, photograph capture and outcome, identical in behaviour to Screen 10C. |
| Good Units / Damaged Units / Insurance Claim | Counter tiles | Running totals for the carton being inspected. |
| Print Product / Location Details | Action with printer icon | Prints the product and location detail slip for the carton. |
| iForU / Proceed to BIN / BIN LATER | Action buttons | The same routing options offered in Case 1C - issue for usage, bin now, or defer to the bin later queue. |
| Cancel | Action | Abandons the carton without recording an outcome. |
| Single product code warning | Warning banner | Repeats the rule that only one product code is allowed inside one primary carton and that the operator cannot proceed otherwise. |
[/TABLE]
{Heading2} 18.5 Screen Field Reference - Screen 10G (Binning Action)
Note: Screen 10G differs from Screen 10D in that location and product are selected and confirmed rather than scanned, since no scannable code exists on either.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |
| Product summary | Read-only panel | Product code, description, manually entered quantity with its good and damaged split, binning zone and recommended binning location. |
| Step 1: Select / Scan Location | Selection or scan field | The recommended location is pre-filled from master data and can be selected rather than scanned, since scanning is unavailable in this case. |
| Location Verified | Status tag | Confirms that the location selection has been accepted. |
| Step 2: Confirm Product | Confirmation field | The product is confirmed rather than scanned, and a success message is displayed. |
| Binning verified successfully | Confirmation panel | States how many units will be binned and how many are set aside for insurance. |
| Confirm BIN | Primary action | Commits the binning transaction. |
| Cancel Carton | Secondary action | Abandons the carton at the binning step. |
| No Location Alert | Warning panel | Offers Enter Location where role-based access allows and Notify Admin otherwise. |
[/TABLE]
{Heading2} 18.6 Screen Field Reference - Screen 10H (Exception / Next State)
Note: Screen 10H is not a step in the main sequence. It presents the two states the flow can enter when a carton cannot be completed normally, and the actions available from each.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |
| State 1: No Location Alert | State panel | Raised where no mapped location exists for the selected product in the zone; presents Enter Location and Notify Admin as the two available responses. |
| Enter Location / Notify Admin | Action buttons | Enter Location is offered only where role-based access permits manual entry; otherwise the request goes to the administrator. |
| State 2: Bin Later / Next Selection State | State panel | Confirms that the current carton has been marked Bin Later, that product and location details have been cleared, and that the next product in the carton can now be identified. |
| Identify Next Product Manually | Action button | Starts manual identification of the next product without leaving the carton. |
| Bin Later Queue | Navigation row with count | Opens the deferred queue and shows the number of cartons pending. |
| Binning later note | Information banner | States that binning of deferred cartons is carried out through the Binning module. |
[/TABLE]
 [IMG]
Figure 18.6a - Case 1D board (Screens 10E to 10H): manual product identification with the scan controls visibly disabled, review and action, binning by location selection rather than scan, and the exception and next-state panel.
{Heading1} 19. FR-016: Case Selection by Supplier-Product Combination and Cross-Cutting Condition Rules
{Heading2} 19.1 Overview
Two rules were confirmed during this session that apply across every case in the direct binning and consignment check flows rather than to any single screen. They are recorded here once so that they are not restated in each case.
The first is that the applicable case is not chosen by the operator at run time. It is defined in advance at supplier and product level, and the moment a supplier and product are selected the system presents only the screens belonging to that case. All other screens are suppressed. The second is that condition capture defaults to OK and is presented collapsed, because in practice the overwhelming majority of products arrive in good condition and asking the operator to select a condition on every unit would slow the check without adding information.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Case Selection - Which of these situations applies - everything scannable, only the inner item scannable, nothing scannable - depends on the supplier and the part, and it does not change from day to day. So the system should work it out itself from what it already knows, rather than asking the operator to choose. This matters because the operator is the worst person to ask. He is standing at a truck, he has forty cartons to move, and the categories mean nothing to him. Ask him to pick one and he will pick the same one every time regardless of what is in front of him, and the whole scheme quietly stops working. Determined automatically it is invisible and reliable; left to the operator it is a source of wrong data that nobody notices for months.
Example: A consignment arrives from a supplier whose cartons are always barcoded but whose inner items never are. The operator scans the first carton. The system recognises the supplier and the part, works out which situation this is, and presents the right screen - sticker printing enabled, inner scanning disabled. He is never asked to categorise anything and could not tell you afterwards that a decision had been made at all.
Condition Capture - Most boxes are fine. If the system asks the operator to state the condition of every single one from a blank field, he has to make forty-eight positive selections to record forty-eight boxes that were all perfectly normal. Starting on OK means he only touches the condition control for the two boxes that actually have something wrong. This is a small design decision with a large effect on whether the data is any good. A field that must be filled for every item, when the answer is nearly always the same, gets filled with whatever is quickest - and after a week that is whatever happens to be first in the list, whether or not it is true. Defaulting to the normal case keeps the exceptions meaningful.
Example: Forty-eight boxes come off the truck. The operator scans each one and does nothing about condition for forty-six of them, because OK is already selected. For box 17 he taps packing torn and for box 31 wet. Two deliberate actions rather than forty-eight, and the two that were recorded are the two that actually mattered.
{Heading2} 19.2 Functional Requirements
FR-016.1: Screen configuration shall be driven by the combination of supplier and product. The applicable case shall be defined at supplier-product level in advance, and on selection of that supplier and product the system shall present only the screens belonging to the applicable case and shall suppress all others.
FR-016.2: The operator shall not be required to select the case at run time; the case shall be resolved by the system from the supplier-product configuration.
FR-016.3: Condition capture shall default to OK on every screen on which it appears, and shall be presented in a collapsed panel. The operator shall expand the panel and record a different condition only where a problem is physically observed.
FR-016.4: The field labelled MOQ shall be relabelled as quantity per pack size across all screens on which it appears. It denotes the number of units in one sale pack and is master data.
FR-016.5: Product, location and related reference data shall be uploaded from the back end as master data and shall be available to every case flow on scan or on manual selection.
{Heading1} 20. FR-017: Video Recording of Carton Opening
{Heading2} 20.1 Overview
A requirement for video evidence of carton opening was raised during this session. The practice it is modelled on is one already followed by dealers, who record the opening, counting and closing of every carton so that a dispute over contents can be settled from the recording. The same practice is used for high-value items and for return consignments, where photographic and video evidence is captured as a matter of routine.
The recording is to be optional and controlled by a toggle available at the point a secondary carton check is started. Because retained video occupies storage, the system is to prompt at the end of the exercise for a decision on whether the recording is kept or discarded.
Note: this requirement is not yet reflected on any wireframe screen. It extends the photographic evidence capture already specified for box and unit condition, and is supplementary to it rather than a replacement. The trigger point, the retention prompt and the storage policy are recorded as an open item.
{Heading2} Layman Description
A video recording of a carton being opened, made when the contents are likely to be disputed. It differs from photographs in showing the sequence - that the carton was sealed when the recording started, that nothing was added or removed, and what was actually inside. That continuity is what a photograph cannot demonstrate. It is optional by design, because recording every carton would produce hours of footage nobody will ever watch and fill the storage on the device. What matters is the decision afterwards: the system should ask whether to keep the recording or discard it, so that the few genuinely needed are retained deliberately and the rest are not left accumulating.
Example: A high-value consignment of ECUs arrives with a broken seal. The storekeeper records the opening on video, showing the seal, the carton being cut, and the six units inside - one of which is missing. At the end the system asks whether to keep it and he keeps it. The next carton is routine, he records nothing, and the day produces one video rather than forty.
{Heading2} 20.2 Functional Requirements
FR-017.1: The system shall support video recording of the carton opening and checking activity, triggered from the point at which the operator begins to open the box.
FR-017.2: Video recording shall be optional and shall be controlled by a toggle presented when a secondary carton check is started.
FR-017.3: At the end of the exercise the system shall prompt the operator to decide whether the recording is retained or deleted, since retained recordings consume storage.
FR-017.4: The retain or delete decision shall be capable of being taken per carton or at the end of the entire consignment check, depending on whether the shipment document details are available.
FR-017.5: Retained recordings shall be held against the carton so that they can be produced as evidence of contents in support of a claim against the supplier or the transporter.
FR-017.6: Video recording shall be supplementary to the photographic evidence already captured against box and unit condition and shall not replace it.
{Heading1} 21. FR-018: Open Delivery Flow - Case 1, Same SKU - Piece-to-Piece Inspection of Cartons Marked at Unloading
{Heading2} 21.1 Overview
Cartons that were flagged during unloading as damaged, tampered or otherwise suspect are marked for Open Delivery at that point and are not binned on the normal path. They are held back and inspected piece by piece before any unit is placed in a bin. This flow covers that inspection for Case 1, where the primary and secondary cartons are the same and the carton holds a single SKU.
The operator picks the carton from the open delivery queue, sees the observation recorded against it at unloading, verifies the product, and then inspects each unit in the pack individually. Good pieces proceed to binning; damaged pieces are set aside for insurance and are excluded from the bin transaction. Where a different product code is found inside the carton the carton is moved to Check Carton, because Case 1 permits only one SKU.
Note: this is the Open Delivery tab that appears alongside Current Check on the consignment check screens. The count badge on the tab is the number of cartons awaiting inspection, so the operator can see the open delivery backlog without leaving the current check.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Open Delivery Queue - Boxes that arrived visibly damaged are not put away with everything else. They go onto a separate list to be opened and examined, usually in front of the driver, so that responsibility is settled before he leaves. The list shows how many are waiting, how many have been dealt with, and - importantly - what was noticed about each one when it came off the truck. Carrying that original observation through is what makes the list usable an hour later, when the man who unloaded it has gone. Without it, somebody is looking at a carton knowing only that something was wrong with it, with no idea whether it was wet, crushed or had a broken seal, and no way to tell whether the damage has got worse since.
Example: Three cartons come off the truck damaged and go onto the open delivery list. At 2 pm the storekeeper opens the list and sees carton 17 marked packing torn, carton 31 wet, carton 40 seal broken. He deals with the seal-broken one first because that is a security matter, and he knows which it is without asking anyone - the man who unloaded it went home at noon.
Piece Inspection - Opening a damaged carton means examining every piece inside it, not glancing at the contents and forming an impression. The system presents them one at a time and counts as it goes: five of twelve checked, and a result recorded against each. Doing it piece by piece is what turns an impression into evidence. A note saying carton damaged, some items affected is worth nothing in a claim. Nine good, three cracked, out of a pack of twelve, with each one recorded, is worth the money. It also stops the two failure modes that happen when there is no structure - condemning a whole carton because the top item was broken, and passing a whole carton because the top item was fine.
Example: Carton 31 should hold twelve alternators and arrived wet. The system presents them one by one. Nine are dry inside their inner packing and pass. Three have water damage. The screen shows 12 of 12 checked, 9 good, 3 damaged. Without the piece-by-piece walk-through the operator would probably have written off all twelve, and nine sellable units would have gone onto a claim.
{Heading2} 21.2 Functional Requirements
FR-018.1: The consignment check screens shall carry two tabs, Current Check and Open Delivery, with a badge on the Open Delivery tab showing the number of cartons pending inspection.
FR-018.2: The Open Delivery Boxes screen shall display counters for Total Open Delivery, Pending Inspection, Ready to BIN and Set Aside / Insurance.
FR-018.3: The screen shall present a searchable and filterable list of cartons showing box number, the observation recorded at unloading, the current status and the available action.
FR-018.4: The observation recorded during unloading - damaged, visible scratch, packing tampered or product OK - shall be carried forward and displayed against the carton so that the inspector knows why it was held back.
FR-018.5: On selecting a carton the system shall display the earlier unload observation together with the Open Delivery Marked flag.
FR-018.6: The system shall require the product in the carton to be scanned or verified. Case 1 shall allow only one SKU in the carton; where a different product code is found the carton shall be moved to Check Carton.
FR-018.7: Inspection shall be performed piece by piece. The system shall present each unit in turn with its condition options - OK, Damaged, Packing Tampered, Visible Scratch - together with photograph capture and an insurance claim checkbox.
FR-018.8: The system shall maintain running totals of Total Units against pack size, Inspected and Remaining, and shall allow the operator to move between units.
FR-018.9: Each unit shall carry an outcome of Proceed to BIN or Set Aside / Insurance, and the screen shall maintain counters for Good Units, Damaged Units and Insurance Claim.
FR-018.10: The binning screen shall show the recommended bin location together with the available alternative locations, and shall require a two-step confirmation of location and product before the bin transaction is committed.
FR-018.11: Only good units shall be binned. Damaged pieces shall remain in Set Aside / Insurance and shall be excluded from the bin transaction, and the system shall state this on screen.
FR-018.12: The flow shall provide the actions Inspect Selected Box, Proceed to Inspection, Set Aside Carton, Proceed Good Units to BIN and Set Aside Damaged Units.
{Heading2} 21.3 Screen Field Reference - Open Delivery Boxes
Note: this is the queue screen. It exists so that cartons held back at unloading are not lost and can be worked through as a distinct backlog, with the reason each was held visible in the list.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |

{Heading1} 22. FR-019: Multi-SKU Consignment Check - Primary Carton Containing Several SKUs - Screens 11a &amp; 11b
{Heading2} 22.1 Overview
The direct binning cases covered so far assume a single part in the primary carton, because the primary and secondary cartons were the same. This flow covers the case where a primary carton holds several different SKUs - a carton that may be filled entirely with one part, or may hold a mix of springs, pistons and other items packed together.
The primary box is scanned once and then stays constant while the operator scans each secondary product inside it in turn, entering the quantity found for each. A live status panel tracks how many units have been checked in that box, how many remain, and how the checked units split between acceptable and not acceptable.
The expected quantity for the box comes from the ship list, which is uploaded at gate entry and states which box contains what and in what quantity. The reconciliation performed here is against that ship list for the box currently open - it is not a reconciliation against the purchase order. Purchase order matching is a separate flow: the shortfall against the order is established once the whole consignment has been checked, and the order may subsequently be closed short.
Note: the wireframe board for this flow is headed Direct to BIN, which does not describe what the screens do. The flow is a multi-SKU consignment check, not direct binning. The heading correction is recorded as an open item.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Multi-SKU Check (FR-019.1 to .8) - A large outer carton often holds several different parts rather than a quantity of one. Checking it means opening it and scanning each part inside separately, then comparing what was found against what the paperwork said should be in there. While the operator works through it, the screen keeps a live tally: how many lines have been checked, how many are still to go, how many were fine and how many had a problem. The running count is what makes it usable. Opening a carton with 30 different parts and no on-screen progress means the operator loses his place the moment he is interrupted, and either starts again or skips something. Seeing 18 of 30 done tells him exactly where he is, and tells a supervisor walking past how far the job has got.
Example: Carton 4 holds 30 different parts. The operator scans them one by one; the screen shows 18 checked, 12 to go, 17 fine, 1 not fine. He is called away for ten minutes, comes back, and picks up at part 19 without recounting. At the end the carton shows 29 found against 30 expected, and the missing line is identified by part number rather than as one item short somewhere in the box.
Multi-SKU Check (FR-019.9 to .11) - The expected quantities have to come from somewhere, and there are only two possibilities: the supplier tells you, or somebody in your warehouse types them in. The difference matters enormously. If the supplier sends a packing list and it is loaded into the system, the expected figures are independent of your own staff and can be used to hold the supplier to what he said he sent. If your own storekeeper types them from the invoice, then a typing error becomes a shortage that never existed, and the supplier can reasonably dispute it. So the question to ask is not just whether expected quantities exist on screen, but where they came from - and whether the check is performed against that list rather than against something assembled locally.
Example: The supplier emails a packing list before dispatch; it is loaded and shows 30 lines totalling 214 pieces. The check is run against those figures and comes back 2 pieces short on one line. Because the expected number came from the supplier own document, the claim is straightforward. Where no packing list is provided, the system says so rather than letting the storekeeper type a number that would then look like independent evidence.
Status Lookup - The same lookup that reports unloading status, used during the checking stage and answering a narrower question: has this particular part been checked yet? It exists because checking a large consignment takes hours and people keep asking about individual parts while it is going on. Rather than interrupting the man doing the checking, anyone can search and see for themselves. In practice it is used with a part number far more often than a box number, because the people asking - the workshop, the parts counter - think in parts, not in cartons. They do not know or care which box something arrived in. A system that only accepts a box number here technically has the feature but will not actually be used, which is exactly the sort of thing that separates a genuine yes from a partial one.
Example: The parts counter has a customer waiting for a clutch plate that was on this morning delivery. Rather than walking to the checking bay, the counter staff type the part number and see it has already been checked and is fine. They promise the customer within the hour. Had the search only accepted a carton number, they would have had no way to look - nobody at the counter knows which carton a clutch plate came in.
{Heading2} 22.2 Functional Requirements
FR-019.1: Where a primary carton contains more than one SKU the multi-SKU consignment check flow shall be used in place of the direct binning flow.
FR-019.2: Screen 11a shall autofill the consignment header from the gate entry number and shall provide Scan Primary Box and Scan Product Code controls, each with a delete control so that an entry captured in error can be removed.
FR-019.3: Once the primary box has been scanned the operator shall scan each secondary product inside that box in turn. The primary box shall remain constant while products within it are scanned.
FR-019.4: The quantity of each scanned product shall be enterable through a stepper control, and the pack size shall be displayed alongside it from master data.
FR-019.5: The product description shall autofill from the scanned product code, and products flagged as fragile in the master shall be highlighted with a fragile indicator.
FR-019.6: The screen shall display the binning zone and location for the scanned product and shall allow the location label to be printed.
FR-019.7: Where the product is routed to a workshop job rather than to stock, the vehicle number shall be captured and shall be printable.
FR-019.8: Screen 11b shall maintain a live Box / Units Check status showing Checked, Balance, OK and Not OK for the box currently being checked.
FR-019.9: The expected quantity for each box shall be taken from the ship list uploaded at gate entry, which states which primary box contains which items and in what quantity.
FR-019.10: The balance shall be calculated against the ship list quantity for the box currently being checked, box by box, and not across all boxes in the consignment together.
FR-019.11: The reconciliation performed on this screen shall be against the ship list only. Reconciliation against the purchase order is a separate flow, and the check shall not compare the scanned quantity to the ordered quantity at this point.
FR-019.12: The system shall provide a status lookup that accepts either a box number or a part number, so that at any stage the operator can establish whether a given carton has been opened and checked.
FR-019.13: Lookup by part number shall be the primary use of the status check, since the operator most often needs to know the status of a part rather than of a carton.
FR-019.14: The flow shall provide the actions Cancel, Check Next Primary Box and Finish Consignment Check, and shall provide access to the variance summary.
{Heading2} 22.3 Screen Field Reference - Screen 11a (Scan Primary Box and Products)
Note: Screen 11a is a capture screen. The delete controls beside each scan field exist because the operator is scanning repeatedly into the same carton and a mis-scan must be removable without abandoning the carton.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |
| Consignment Details | Collapsible section | Gate entry number, supplier and document number, autofilled for the consignment being checked. |
| Scan Primary BOX | Scan or select field with delete control | Captures the primary box; the delete control removes a box captured in error. |
| Scan Product Code | Scan or select field with delete control | Captures each secondary product found inside that primary box; the primary box stays constant while products are scanned. |
| Quantity | Stepper control | Number of units of the scanned product found in this box. |
| MOQ | Read-only field | Quantity per pack size, from the product master. |

| Location Details - Zone / Location | Read-only fields with print control | Binning zone and location for the scanned product, printable as a label. |
| Deliver to Workshop - Vehicle Number | Field with print control | Captured where the product is routed to a workshop job rather than to stock. |
[/TABLE]
{Heading2} 22.4 Screen Field Reference - Screen 11b (Check Status and Actions)
Note: Screen 11b is the status and control panel for the carton currently open. The counters refer to the open carton only, not to the consignment as a whole.
[TABLE]

 [IMG]
Figure 22.4a - Multi-SKU consignment check (Screens 11a and 11b): primary box and product scanning with delete controls, quantity and pack size capture, the fragile flag, and the box and units check status with the check status and check variance actions.
{Heading1} 23. FR-020: Consignment Check Variance Summary - Screen 11c
{Heading2} 23.1 Overview
The variance summary closes the consignment check. It reports the position of the whole consignment in value, part lines and quantity, showing what was invoiced, what was received, what is short and what has been received in excess, with a net position. Alongside it a second table reports the qualitative position - how much of what was received is damaged, tampered, scratched, broken or wrongly delivered.
The reference for the variance is the ship list rather than the invoice, since the ship list is what states which carton held which items. Short quantities are not a closure of the matter: the balance is expected to arrive incrementally on subsequent deliveries, and the purchase order remains open until it is either fulfilled or closed short by the supplier.
Note: Screen 11c is reached from Check Variance on Screen 11b and is activated once Finish Consignment Check has been pressed. The two tables are laid out for entry against a fixed set of rows, so the summary structure is the same for every consignment regardless of its size.
{Heading2} Layman Description
Variance Reporting (FR-020.1 to .3; FR-020.5 to .8) - At the end of a check, one summary that says what should have arrived, what did arrive, and the difference - expressed both in money and in quantity, and broken down by part line. Money matters because that is the language of the claim and of the accounts department. Quantity matters because that is what the warehouse actually deals in. A summary that gives only one of the two forces somebody to work out the other by hand. Short and excess are reported separately rather than netted off, since two lines short and two lines over is a very different situation from everything balancing. It should be printable, because the driver or the supplier will want a copy, and correctable before it is finalised - the first pass through a check always contains a miscount or two.
Example: The check finishes showing invoiced Rs 2,40,000 against received Rs 2,31,500, with 3 lines short totalling 6 pieces and 1 line over by 2 pieces. The storekeeper spots that one short line was actually a miscount, corrects it, and finalises. The summary is printed, signed by the driver, and sent with the claim - a single sheet showing both the money and the pieces.
Variance Reporting (FR-020.4; FR-021.17) - Not all problems are the same problem. A carton with a scratch on it is a different matter from a carton whose seal has been tampered with, and both are different from the wrong item having been sent altogether. This reports each kind separately rather than lumping them into a single count of things that were wrong. The reason is that each kind goes to a different place. Transit damage is an insurance matter; tampered packaging is a security matter and may involve the transporter; wrong delivery is a supplier error with no claim attached at all, just a return. If the summary says only 5 problems, somebody has to open every one to find out what to do with them. If it says 3 damaged, 1 tampered, 1 wrong item, the work sorts itself.  This capability also spans Section 24.
Example: A consignment finishes with 5 problem lines. The summary splits them: 3 damaged in transit, 1 with the packing seal broken, 1 where a different part number was sent. The 3 go to the insurance claim, the tampered carton is raised with the transporter that afternoon, and the wrong part is set aside for return. Three different actions, three different people, sorted from one screen without opening anything again.
{Heading2} 23.2 Functional Requirements
FR-020.1: The system shall provide a variance summary screen, reachable through Check Variance and activated once Finish Consignment Check has been completed.
FR-020.2: The summary shall report value, part lines and quantity against the rows Invoiced, Received, Short, Excess and Net.
FR-020.3: Short shall report the shortfall against the shipment document and Excess shall report quantity received over and above it; the net position shall be reported after both.
FR-020.4: The system shall report quality variance separately by qualitative aspect - Damage, Tampered Packaging, Scratch, Broken and Wrong Delivery - against value, parts and quantity.
FR-020.5: Variance shall be computed with reference to the ship list, which states the number of part line items and the quantity dispatched, rather than against the invoice alone.
FR-020.6: Short quantities shall remain open pending subsequent deliveries. The system shall not treat a shortfall as final, since the balance is commonly delivered incrementally on later dates.
FR-020.7: The variance summary shall be printable and shall be editable before it is finalised.
FR-020.8: Damaged, scratched and broken items shall be reportable by quantity, value and part number so that the claim position is visible from the summary.
{Heading2} 23.3 Screen Field Reference - Screen 11c (Check Variance)
Note: the two tables on this screen answer different questions. The first asks how much was received against how much was sent; the second asks what condition it arrived in.
[TABLE]
| Field | Control Type | Description |
| Summary (Value) | Table | Reports the consignment position across three columns - Value (Rs.), Part Lines (Nos.) and Quantity (Nos.). |
| Invoiced | Table row | Value, part lines and quantity as stated on the shipment document. |
| Received | Table row | Value, part lines and quantity actually checked in during the consignment check. |
| Short | Table row | The shortfall of the received position against the document. |
| Excess | Table row | Quantity received over and above the document. |
| Net | Table row | The net position once short and excess have been applied. |
| Quality Variance (No. of Boxes / Units) | Table | Reports the qualitative position across Value (Rs.), Parts (Nos.) and Qty (Nos.). |
| Damage / Tampered Packaging / Scratch / Broken / Wrong Delivery | Table rows | The qualitative aspects against which the received consignment is reported. |
| Edit control | Action icon | Allows the variance entry to be corrected before it is finalised. |
| Print control | Action icon | Prints the variance summary. |
[/TABLE]
 [IMG]
Figure 23.3a - Screen 11c: the variance summary, with the value summary reporting invoiced, received, short, excess and net across value, part lines and quantity, and the quality variance table reporting by qualitative aspect.
{Heading1} 24. FR-021: Case 2A - Secondary Cartons Inside the Primary Cartons, Both Scannable and Linkage Available - Screens 11A, 11B &amp; 11C
{Heading2} 24.1 Overview
Case 2A covers the arrangement in which secondary cartons sit inside the primary carton. The primary carton is opened and a number of separately packed items are found within it. Both the primary and the secondary cartons are scannable, the supplier details have been uploaded, and the primary-secondary linkage is available - so the system knows in advance which secondary packs belong in which primary carton and in what quantity.
Because the linkage is available, the check is a reconciliation rather than a discovery exercise. The primary box is opened and the system states how many secondary packs are expected in it. Each secondary carton is then scanned, the count for each SKU is tracked against what was expected, and the balance is visible throughout. Once the box is finished the operator moves to the next primary carton, and on completion the variance is reported carton by carton as well as across the consignment.
One control shown on the original board is to be removed. The screen prompted the operator where a scanned primary box was not found in the uploaded list, asking whether to proceed. That situation does not arise at consignment check, because cartons that do not belong to the consignment are already identified and segregated during unloading. By the time the consignment check begins, every carton in front of the operator belongs to this consignment.
Note: the Case 2A board carried no screen numbers when it was first presented. Session 06 re-presented the same board in higher fidelity with the numbering shown on it, confirming the three panels as Screens 11A, 11B and 11C - Consignment Check, Scan Secondary Cartons and Check Variance. The numbering below is therefore taken from the source and is no longer analyst-assigned.
{Heading2} Layman Description
This is the easiest situation to handle. The big outer carton has a barcode, the smaller boxes inside it also have barcodes, and the supplier has already told you which small boxes belong in which big one. Because that relationship is known in advance, the system can be specific: it knows carton A should contain boxes 1 to 6, so when only five are scanned it can say precisely which one is missing, from which carton. That is much stronger than knowing you are one box short somewhere in a load of two hundred. The relationship comes from the supplier, not from anything your warehouse does, so the feature only works where the supplier provides it - which is why the next few rows deal with what happens when he does not.
Example: Outer carton A is scanned and the system shows it should hold 6 boxes. The operator scans 5 and the sixth never appears. The screen names it - box number 4 of carton A - and the shortage is reported against that specific box. The supplier cannot argue about which load or which carton, because the record points at his own packing information.
{Heading2} 24.2 Functional Requirements
FR-021.1: Case 2A shall be applied where secondary cartons are contained inside the primary carton, both primary and secondary cartons are scannable, supplier details have been uploaded and the primary-secondary linkage is available.
FR-021.2: The consignment check screen shall carry two tabs, Consignment Check and Direct to BIN, so that the operator can move between the full check and the direct binning path for the same consignment.
FR-021.3: The primary box shall be captured by scan or selection, and the system shall require that the primary carton exists in the uploaded box list for the selected gate entry number.
FR-021.4: The prompt asking whether to proceed where a primary box is not found in the uploaded list shall be removed from this screen. Cartons that do not belong to the consignment are identified and segregated during unloading, so the situation does not arise at consignment check.
FR-021.5: On opening a primary box the system shall record and display the box reference, its active status and the time at which it was opened.
FR-021.6: The system shall display a work-in-progress summary for the open primary box showing Expected Secondary Packs, Scanned and Balance.
FR-021.7: Each secondary carton shall be captured by scanning its product or carton code, with a manual and predictive search alternative for codes that cannot be read because they are smudged or damaged.
FR-021.8: On a successful secondary scan the system shall display the product code, description, pack size, binning zone, binning location and workshop vehicle number, together with the fragile flag and the workshop required flag where they apply.
FR-021.9: The system shall maintain a current count for the SKU being scanned, with manual entry available, and shall show progress in the form of units scanned so far against units expected.
FR-021.10: The system shall maintain a table of all products scanned in the open primary box showing product code, description, expected quantity, scanned quantity, balance and binning zone.
FR-021.11: Where a product not previously seen in this box is scanned the counter shall start at one. Where a product already scanned in this box is scanned again later the counter shall continue from its last recorded count rather than restarting.
FR-021.12: Condition shall be captured per SKU against the standard condition set, with photograph capture and an insurance claim flag.
FR-021.13: The flow shall provide the actions Issue for Usage, Proceed to BIN and Scan Next Product at SKU level, and Check Next Primary Box, Refresh WIP Status, Check Product Status and Finish Consignment Check at box and consignment level.
FR-021.14: The system shall provide a product status lookup which, on entry of a product code, returns the primary box, the scanned quantity, the expected quantity, the balance and the time at which the product was last scanned.
FR-021.15: The variance screen shall be activated once Finish Consignment Check has been completed and shall report Total SKUs Expected, Total Units Expected and Units Scanned.
FR-021.16: Because the primary-secondary linkage is available in this case, variance shall be reported carton by carton, showing for each primary box the product code, description, expected quantity and actual quantity found.
FR-021.17: Quality variance shall be reported across the consignment by qualitative aspect - damaged, packing tampered and visible scratch.
FR-021.18: The system shall confirm on screen that the consignment check has been completed for the primary box, and shall allow the variance to be printed.
{Heading2} 24.3 Screen Field Reference - Screen 11A (Consignment Check - Primary Box Capture)
Note: Screen 11A opens one primary carton at a time and states up front how many secondary packs should be found inside it, which is what makes the check a reconciliation rather than a discovery exercise.
